# RMBP — WHAT IS IN FLIGHT AND WHAT IS OWED (the x86 rMBP track queue, R45)

One file, fixed path, not per-round. Kept current AS WORK HAPPENS, never at close. Only jobs that need
the rMBP's metal or x86-only files live here (`arch/x86_64`, Kepler/gmux/EHCI/FTDI, the x86 specs,
`stage-x86.sh`); everything else is a row in the trunk queue `docs/dev/QUEUE.md` on `main`. Ledger:
`docs/dev/OS/rmbp-ledger.md` (A/B/E) and `docs/dev/LEDGER.md` (SR<n>). `✓` = verified in this tree ·
`·` = inherited, not re-checked. Re-derive every sha before acting.
Created 2026-09-12T00:35Z (2026-09-11 local; dates here are UTC) by orin 27 (all lanes, R46). rmbp 19's own measurement, quoted by Peter the same day:
"88 open rows is a graveyard, not a queue" and "16 items … exist only in a plans directory outside the
repo" — this file is the queue those two sentences asked for; the 16 baton items were not read (R37),
their three named subjects are rows here or in the trunk queue.

The tell was `max_ms=0` on every failing capture against `max_ms=2..25` on greens — the flakes are the FAST runs — and `[wcser] scope=fixture declined_pct=23..31` beside each one, free on every capture all along. FIXED fixture-only (`dmgovlp_selftest` routes every composite through a bounded `composite_live` that asks `WCSER_DECLINED` whether the call took the gate, waits the holder out and retries; wall REPORTED on a new `[dmgovlp] serialisation folds= fold_ms= unran=` line and never gated, LAWS §5; the per-pass stretch line now names the miss with `why=`). FIXTURE_FLAKES §1c re-classed and closed.
· MENUSTAT (branch exec-rmbp-menustat, parent e4d0494c; the branch tip is the sha — the seat fills it at the fold; ledger row **B148**) — **THE MENU BAR'S STATUS AREA HAS ITS FIRST ITEM: THE BATTERY, LEFT OF THE CLOCK, IN THE MAC POSITION (R25 / LAWS §6).** The decode is fed flight 11's own scout bytes (`BRSC=[00 52]` = 82 %, `B0AC=[03 f6]` = +1014 mA, `B0AV=[30 17]` = 12311 mV, `B0TF=[00 6a]` = 106 min), and **`charging` is the `B0AC` SIGN, not a `B0St` bit** — `B0St=[00 80]` is one sample in one state and cannot falsify any bit assignment, while `B0AC`'s sign is IVY-AC's metal-confirmed claim that `derive_ac` already ships on; it is read and reported raw and decides nothing until a flight records it in both states. The bar reads a new shared model, `video/status.rs`, and **never a driver** — `drivers::smc` is x86-only and `menubar.rs` compiles on the Pi, so the item is `battery`, its source is `status::Source`, and the source is resolved **by measurement**: QEMU's `isa-applesmc` carries no battery key, so that boot's item is the MEASURED absence and says so — `[menubar] battery absent src=none`. Layout is inside the EXISTING floors (`FLOOR_W` untouched — widening it would take the bar off panels on machines with no battery at all): item_w=68, glyph=26x12, inner gap=6, percent right-aligned in 4 glyphs, item x=1143 one PAD left of the clock, and `clock=45x20+1223+7` / `caption_x0=40` / `floor=118x110` **identical with the item present and absent**. The poll is 10 s on the device-service task because one SMC transaction is ~200 us measured (flight 11: 493 index reads in 99 ms), and the damage signature folds only what is DRAWN, measured off `compose()`'s own return: `jitter_paint=false change_paint=true`. GATES: `./arroyo knoboff wc e4d0494c` **exit 0** and `./arroyo knoboff smc e4d0494c` **exit 0**, both `warm=yes`, control fired on both arches; `UNAOS_WC=1 UNAOS_QUARRY=1 UNAOS_FTDIRX=1 UNAOS_SMC=1 UNAOS_QEMU_FULL=1 ./arroyo test 240` **rc 0 COMPLETE** (line 2496, wall 240.9 s, MBENCH 6/6); `:: MENUBATT: … decode_ok=true gone_red=true red_pct=100 layout_ok=true damage_ok=true :: PASS ::` with the go-red (a byte swap armed inside the decoder) in the same passing run; `[menubar] battery pct=` and `[menubar] battery absent src=` both present in the non-`witness` `esp-x86` kernel by `LC_ALL=C grep -a -o -F`, and `:: MENUBATT:`/`:: MENUBAR:` absent from it. ⚠ OWED, named not taken: the `:: MENUBATT:` REQUIRE + FAIL/SKIP FORBIDs. `arroyo test` replays `x86-default.spec` and `x86-wc.spec` is the `mbench` verb's; no menubar line this arc touches is pinned in either, so neither spec was edited. ⚠ One middle run reddened `:: DOCKID: … set=false … :: FAIL ::` — verbatim the flake **QUEUE.md:93** (PARTINSTALL) registered, the one sub-assertion that moves, on a tree the dock cannot read; the lone re-run LAWS §5 prescribes cleared it.

· FTDICR (branch `exec-rmbp-ftdicr`, parent 4638014e; the sha is the commit carrying this clause — a self-referencing sha cannot be written into its own commit, so the seat folds it at the land) — **DOCS ONLY: NO KERNEL BYTES MOVE, because the brief's fix was the wrong subsystem and the capture says so.** The job was "make `\r` from the FTDI RX path produce the same Enter the keyboard produces, in the smallest place: `drivers/xhci/ftdi.rs`'s byte->PAL-event mapping". MEASURED FIRST on `~/unaos-bench/capture/rmbp12-flight8/ttyUSB0.log` after `=== SQUAWK MARK flight10` (read-only, awk index(), the flight untouched): `[quarry] key_route key=0x0d focus=1 took=1` at 252772ms and 256841ms — **a focused Quarry ate the CR** — and `key=0x0d focus=0 took=0` at 375912ms, 19 ms before `:: [midden] cmd="helpdatehelp" -> TerminalError len=44 ::`. THE SAME BYTE SUBMITTED THE LINE once focus was gone, so `\r` was Enter all along (`handle_key` in main.rs takes CR or LF) and the keyboard was never privileged — both of its Enters that flight (`key=0x0a`, 143793ms and 396387ms) arrived with `focus=0`. Quarry's arm matches CR and LF alike, so a CR->LF map at the FTDI intake fixes NOTHING: it was written, gated and then reverted, and the reverted diff is quoted whole in the FTDICR commit body rather than trashed (R20). THE GO-RED PROVED THE SAME THING INDEPENDENTLY and is why nothing was shipped: the QEMU fixture answered `cmd="help" -> TerminalOutput len=3793` BOTH with the arm and with it disabled (`arroyo test 90` rc=0 and `ftdi_inject.py` rc=0 on both legs; TX unregressed, `:: U2.5: FTDI TX mirror -> PASS (142100 boot bytes replayed) ::`), because QEMU takes no Kepler takeover and never focuses Quarry while flight 10 is `build=kepler+takeover+fifo+ivb+wc+smc+`. A check that cannot fire is an absent one (LAWS §5). **THE REAL JOB, for whoever takes it: an operator at the serial console cannot reach the shell at all while Quarry holds focus, and is given no way to see why.** It lives in `unaos/crates/kernel/src/video/quarry/live.rs` (the CR-or-LF arm of `key_route`, which returns consumed unconditionally) and in the key-door ORDER at `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs`'s `wc_route_event` — a file two concurrent executors already hold, so FTDICR touched neither and reports instead (EXECUTOR-BRIEF §3). Whether Enter belongs to a focused Quarry or to the shell is a GLASS question for Peter, not an executor's to decide; what is not in doubt is that the console must be reachable. **Any fixture for it must FOCUS QUARRY FIRST** — every existing QEMU RX leg scores a state in which the defect cannot occur. rmbp-ledger A9's status carries the same finding.

· EHCIDARK (branch exec-rmbp-ehcidark, parent eccac4bb; the branch tip is the sha — the seat fills it at the fold; ledger row B146) — **THE PASS PERIOD *IS* THE DARK WINDOW, AND ON FLIGHT 11 IT WAS ALREADY THE TICK — `max=108ms` IS A TAIL AND THE TAIL IS THE SERIAL CONSOLE.** TWO BRIEFS WERE REFUSED BY MEASUREMENT BEFORE THIS ONE LANDED. QEMU could not have caught it: QEMU is chain-mode, the opposite fork from the metal. And the 1 ms TICK TASK **is already built**: `[deadman] pmp=` gives p50 = 965 passes/s (1.04 ms) over the 928 seconds after the scheduler comes up, because `x86_usb_pump` sleeps one tick on service core c7 while render owns c1 (`SCHED-X86 PLACE: aps=7 rsvc=c1 svc=c7`) — **B134's rule that the input band must never wait on the compositor is structurally satisfied on this path and was never the term.** What is left is a TAIL: **1.08 ms mean pass period across the 840 serial-QUIET seconds against 6.46 ms across the 17 BURST seconds**, min 38 passes in the worst second. `serial::_print` masks interrupts and drains its staging ring through a 16550 a byte at a time; the deadman's own 1 Hz line stretches to 2041 ms in the same seconds **on a different core**, so it is machine-wide, not a slow pump body. If [1] does chain-fetch, §33b's blocker is void for the one endpoint that matters. **A wrong answer HSEs the controller carrying the internal keyboard AND trackpad, losing both for that boot**, so it must not run unattended. **FLIGHT 12 SCORES:** `PASSPERIOD self-test … -> PASS`; `pass_period_us_mean` at ~1000 us with `pass_period_us_max` in the tens of thousands tracking the burst seconds. Mechanism and both residuals: `docs/dev/OS/07_USB_STORAGE/usb_xhci.md` §33h

✓ R8CAP (branch `exec-rmbp-r8cap` off 2ce3c103, gen7.rs `mod r8` under `#[cfg(feature = "gen7r8")]` + gen7.md §2.8): flights 8/9 refused R8 at `dst_bytes=1064960 max=1048576` and the ceiling was the defect, not the panel. `R8_DST_MAX_BYTES` was the literal `1024*1024` under a comment claiming a 1920x1200 bench panel asking 123 pages; the bench rMBP is 2880x1800 at the firmware's padded 4096-px stride (`:: KDHEAD: gop w=2880 h=1800 pitch=16384`, and `fb_len=29491200 = 1800*16384` confirms it independently) and asks 260 — so 16384 B/row is the REAL scanout pitch, not an over-estimate. The ceiling's actual job was sizing the fixed PTE table `[0u32; 1+4+(R8_DST_MAX_BYTES/4096)]` = 261 entries against a `win_slots` of 265: four short, and nothing in the file said the constant was an array bound. NEW CAP, derived: `R8_WIN_SLOTS_MAX = 1 + R8_SRC_PAGES + R8_DST_MAX_PAGES` = 1+4+260 = **265 slots**, with `R8_DST_MAX_BYTES = R8_DST_MAX_PAGES*4096` = **1 064 960 B** falling out of it and `R8_SRC_PAGES`/`R8_DST_ROWS` derived from the rectangle under two `const assert!`s. The one choice left is `R8_MAX_STRIDE_PX = 4096`, taken from the KDHEAD line. COST, measured: 4 B/slot of stack — `fb_blit`'s frame goes 3240 -> ~3256 B (+16), where reserving instead for BR13's widest encodable pitch (1041 pages, 1046 slots) would have cost 4184 B of table and a ~6.4 KiB frame, and **no function in this kernel's x86 artifact has a frame above 4096 B** (`objdump -d` over every `sub $imm,%rsp`). The refusal is KEPT and stays reachable (a 4097..16383-px stride clears the pitch refusal and lands on it), now printing `rows= pitch= stride_px= max_stride_px= slots_have= slots_need= short_by=` so the next shortfall is legible from the wire instead of re-derived — flight 8's re-derivation gave 16640 B/row by assuming 64 rows when the rung flies 65. Gate: `./arroyo check` rc=0; NO QEMU (q35 has no Ivy Bridge — the rung cannot execute there, the artifact is the witness). Byte identity: every edit is inside `mod r8`, which runs to EOF, so a knob-off image is untouched by construction. GEN7R8 FLIGHT in METAL below is unchanged and is now flyable.

MECHANISM of the second, which survives arming the knob: **x86 has no door to Quarry at all.** `quarry::open()` is called only from `desktop_firmware::activate()` step 6 (`video/desktop_firmware.rs:414`) and `quarry::service()` — the drain for the dock tile's reopen latch — only from `main.rs:8898` and `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs:14324`, all three `desktop_firmware`-gated, and `desktop_firmware` is not in `x86-all` (`arroyo:4310`). · W5I2 (branch `exec-rmbp-w5i2`, parent 891c4dec; the sha is that branch's HEAD, stamped at the land like QUARRYDOCK's) — **PCIE-RP-RECOVERY §12.3 I2 is BUILT and NOT WIRED: the NMI probe of the declared-dead core.** `arch::x86_64::interrupts::nmi_probe(core)` (tail block `w5nmi`, cfg `any(witness, bar1wedge)`) sends an NMI IPI through the new bounded `apic::send_nmi_bounded`, waits 10 ms on a per-core record the NMI handler writes (rip, cs; no lock, no print, no GS), and prints `:: W5: nmi core=c<n> taken=<y/n> rip=<hex|-> in_blit=<y/n/?> cs=<hex|-> memcpy=<lo>..<hi> icr=<ok|busy> ::`. `in_blit` is rip-in-`memcpy` — `FrameBuffer::blit` is inlined and its `copy_nonoverlapping` is a call to the 47-byte compiler-builtins `memcpy` whose `rep movs` ARE the aperture stores; the range is `memcpy as *const ()` to its first `ret`, printed on the line. Metal reading after a steal: `taken=n` = hardware-parked, the store is in flight forever, only a reset path can recover the core; `taken=y in_blit=y` = the store completes and the loop does not, software; `taken=y in_blit=n` = the core is elsewhere, the wcser diagnosis is wrong. SEAT'S LINE AT THE FOLD: `crate::arch::interrupts::nmi_probe(dead);` inside W5I1's post-steal hook (either cfg compiles it). Self-test rides `sched::emit_load_witness`'s witness tail (same-line fold at `sched.rs:1560`, file stays 7590 lines), first call = the BSP's `-prejoin`: run 1 `taken=y rip_in_spin=y in_blit=n … -> PASS` and idle `taken=y … -> PASS`; go-red (record stores commented) `taken=n … -> FAIL` rc=1; QEMU cannot produce `taken=n`. Gates: `check` rc=0 (twice, before and after the go-red revert), `knoboff witness 891c4dec` rc=0 warm=yes control fired, `UNAOS_WC=1 UNAOS_QUARRY=1 UNAOS_QEMU_FULL=1 ./arroyo test 120` rc=0 full wall 126.7 s. Evidence `docs/dev/evidence/rmbp-0916/w5i2/SELFTEST.md`; B119 appended. [folded as 792f8331]
· WIFISWEEP (branch `exec-rmbp-wifisweep`, parent f8f8ce8c; sha = that branch's HEAD, stamped here at the land like QUARRYDOCK's — an executor cannot name a sha its own commit does not yet have) — **B113's repair: SECSTA2's post-enumeration sticky clear ran BEFORE the last config walk of the boot, and now runs after it.** MEASURED FIRST, on `~/unaos-bench/capture/rmbp12-flight8/ttyUSB0.log` (copied, read-only): the clear prints at 23263 ms and the wifi census sweeps buses 0..=255 at 23516 ms — 253 ms later — then arc 2's targeted `04:00.0` config accesses run to 23562 ms and **no config-space access of any kind follows until the storm's own `rp-at-wedge` at 331272 ms**. In the source, `grep -rn '0\.\.=255\|0u16\.\.256' crates/kernel/src`: every `0..=255`-shaped walker (`PciScanner::{enumerate_buses,storage_inventory,find_device}`, `full_census`, `detect_gpus`, `ahci::probe`, `ehci::init`, `ehci_scout::{scout,configure_and_relook}`, `bcma::recon`, `vperf::pci_display_probe`) is called from inside `arch::x86_64::pci::init`; `wifi::bus::census` (`wifi/bus.rs:125`, reached from `wifi::service`'s FIRST main-loop pass) is the only one that is not. So the walks are NOT spread across the boot and the brief's `baseline=at-storm` fallback was not taken. BUILT: `sticky_clear_post_enum` is one-shot and its call site moved out of `arch/x86_64/pci.rs:1148` (the line KEPT, statement gone, comment rewritten to say why) into `main.rs:1261`/`:1745`/`:6013`, immediately after `wifi::service()` at the three storage-ready passes — after the last walk on a `wifi` build because the sweep just returned, and on a non-`wifi` build because `wifi::service` is cfg-erased and the point is still below `init_network` → `find_device`. The line gains `site=post-wifi walks_since_enum=<n> walkers=<names>`, counted through the new idempotent `pcihealth::note_wifi_census` at the call site (the wifi arc is forward-only: one sweep per boot, so 1 is the honest count however many times the loop turns), and the no-device case now prints `[pcih] bar1wedge post-enum clear REFUSED reason=no-root-port … site=post-wifi` instead of returning silently — which is the ONLY reason the order is scorable off metal. Docs: `PCIE-RP-RECOVERY.md` §11.5 (with §11.3/§11.4's falsified claims struck in place), SHUTOUT-REGISTER §6 P7 (contract amended), rmbp-ledger B113 → fixed-unflown. No new knob (`bar1wedge`), no new ledger id. Byte identity: `git diff --numstat` = 1/1 on `arch/x86_64/pci.rs` and 6/6 on `main.rs` (at each site `wifi::service()` was folded up onto its own `#[cfg]` line so the two new statements get a line of their own without changing the file's length — GATE-FC2 reads a reference's cfg as the union of every `#[cfg]` on its LINE, and appending beside `wifi::service()` made `pub mod wifi` look declared wider than every path reaching it, `check` rc=1); `pcihealth.rs` grows but is `#[cfg(feature = "nvidia-kepler")]`, so a knob-off image never compiles it. Gate: `./arroyo check` rc=0 (rc=1 first, on the GATE-FC2 finding above; the fold fixed it, no registry entry). QEMU, `UNAOS_WC=1 UNAOS_KEPLER=1 UNAOS_KEPLER_TAKEOVER=1 UNAOS_BAR1WEDGE=1 UNAOS_WIFI=1 UNAOS_QEMU_FULL=1 ./arroyo test 120`: base rc=0, after rc=1, after re-run rc=0 — **THREE runs, not the brief's two, and the extra one was spent on a red the brief's rule (`rc must equal the leg's rc at the base`) would otherwise have left unexplained.** The red is `[dmgovlp]`, quoted in full: base `drag_evt=5 drag_px=38590 relay=3 narrow=3/12 adopt=25 repaint=0 max_ms=5 adopt_stretch=4/4 -> PASS`; after run 1 `drag_evt=0 drag_px=0 relay=0 narrow=0/12 adopt=0 repaint=1 max_ms=1 adopt_stretch=0/4 -> FAIL`; after run 2, SAME TREE, SAME COMMAND, `drag_evt=5 drag_px=38590 relay=3 narrow=3/12 adopt=28 repaint=0 max_ms=5 adopt_stretch=4/4 -> PASS` — every scored field back to the base's value. So the FAIL is the fixture flaking and not this change, which on q35 has no register access at all to flake with (`PCIH_READY=0`: two relaxed atomics and one serial line). Read against trunk §5's DRAGSTALL row — an added serial print inside the ladder DOES cost `dmgovlp` its drag leg — the discriminator is WHERE the print is: DRAGSTALL's rode `dmgovlp_selftest`'s own head, this one is 1321 capture lines earlier on the first main-loop pass. **A rate for FLAKERATE's Class 5, measured here: `[dmgovlp]` 1 red in 2 on this tree at 1-min load 3.97 (`nproc` 20), against its 0-in-4 on `da2a9abc`.** ORDER on q35, which is the field this arc is scored on: at the base NOTHING printed (`awk 'index($0,"[pcih]")'` = 0 hits — the silent return made the order unscorable); after, on BOTH runs, `serial.log:552` is `:: wifi: no Broadcom … parked ::` and `:553` is `[pcih] bar1wedge post-enum clear REFUSED … site=post-wifi walks_since_enum=1 walkers=wifi-census` — the rung below the last config walk, never above it, and `walks_since_enum=1` counted the sweep that q35 still performs before parking. **OWED: the metal reading.** `relatch=` is still unattributed until a flight prints it from the new site.
✓ PTRREPAINT (branch `exec-rmbp-ptrrepaint`, parent f8f8ce8c; the sha is the commit carrying this clause — a self-referencing sha cannot be written into its own commit, so the seat folds it at the land) — **A11's FLICKER half, fixed; the CHOP half is NOT and the row says so.** MEASURED from `~/unaos-bench/capture/rmbp12-flight8/ttyUSB0.log` (read-only, copied to scratch): boot 1's `[cursor11] px_redrawn` is FLAT at 4320 across all ten rollups, 202 s -> 394 s, **0.00 px/s**, `px_installed` 9936 -> 59456 — the WINDOW composite never blinked the arrow under `[wpace] rate=137.5/s`. The blink is the DESKTOP present: `[flick2] flush_undraw=` 19 -> 152 against `flush_skip=` 45 -> 229, i.e. only the ~40 % of presents whose damage MEETS the sprite, each one `cursor::undraw` -> blit -> `cursor::repaint` with the x86 FRONT buffer being the scan-out. Orin control: `flush_undraw=5794 flush_skip=1` and no blink, because `SPRITE_OWNS_PAINT` is false there. FIX: the sprite joins `present_background`'s occluder set (`DESK_SPRITE_OCC`) and `cursor::absorb_desktop` re-saves the withheld pixels from the back buffer. x86 `wc` only; aarch64 arrays element-for-element unchanged, pinned by `const _: () = assert!(!DESK_SPRITE_OCC || SPRITE_OWNS_PAINT)`. **TWO ROWS FALL OUT OF IT, both for whoever picks up A11 next:** (1) boot 2 is a DIFFERENT defect — `px_installed` 0/1008 vs `px_redrawn` 4320 -> 17740, verdict `BRACKETED`, the compose-through not reaching the pixels under the pointer, and the likelier "super choppy"; (2) **x86 HAS NO FIXTURE-SCOPE EMITTER for the whole `[wc-i]`/`[cursor3..11]`/`[flick2]` block** — `pal::cursor::rollup_tick` needs a HID pointer talking for 5 s and the q35 `usb-tablet` delivers ONE report (`:: MOUSE-1: 1 reports`, XHCIHUB `evts=1`), so that block printed ZERO lines on the `f8f8ce8c` gate while aarch64 calls `wm::wci_rollup` from `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs`. PTRREPAINT gives `[cursor11]` a desktop-present cadence (`cursor11_desk_tick`, `scope=desk`) because that is the population its new number describes; giving x86 the fixture-scope call its sibling has is one line in `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` — a file this brief did not name, so it is a JOB here and not a silent edit. Precedent for the move is in that file already: CLICK-ROUTE adopted `hittest_selftest` from the aarch64 site with exactly this argument.
· CHOP (branch `exec-rmbp-chop`, parent `11ca67f1`; docs only — **no kernel bytes move**, so no `knoboff` run and no QEMU witness: R38's one run hosts a fixture and this arc built none) — **STOPPED, AND THE STOP IS THE DELIVERABLE: A11's chop half is a BLITTER STALL, and PTRREPAINT's row (1) above reads the wrong instrument.** `px_installed`/`px_redrawn` are a compose-through SETTLEMENT; `-> BRACKETED` says nothing about how often the arrow moved, so the install path was measured instead on the same capture (`~/unaos-bench/capture/rmbp12-flight8/ttyUSB0.log`, read-only, copied to scratch). NOT the bracket: `[wedge9] sprite-claim … refused=11475 masked=8462 retried=5 owed=1 serviced=2633` over all of flight 8 — `retried=5` counts the bounded UNMASKED retries that SUCCEEDED (`claim_bounded`, cursor.rs:403), so `repaint`'s own claim essentially never contended, and `owed=1 serviced=2633` is the handoff being CASHED rather than lost (`LOST` is the verdict reserved for refusals with nothing cashed and nothing pending). `refused` itself is a MIXED population and does not isolate the motion path — `owe_repaint` (cursor.rs:465) charges it from every call site, and 8462 of the 11475 are the composite side's masked asks — which is why the FOLD counter and not the claim counter is what decides this — and `[cursor8] … requests=2658 repairs=2634 suppressed_rate=18` is 0.7 %. NOT HID-side: `[deadman] hid=` 12–115 reports/s through the thirteen storm seconds the pad was talking, `pmp=` 316–964/s, `[schedx86] depth sent=` 1467 -> 2231 across the storm. **THE INSTALL COLLAPSES:** `GUI_FOLD_X86` (`[schedx86] … fold=`) is **0 for the whole boot to 326403 ms** and **477** by 385196 ms against `sent` +764 — 62 % of the events the channel ACCEPTED during the storm were folded into another and installed nothing of their own (`fold` is Mouse-only while `sent` counts every class, so the motion-only fraction is higher, not lower); worst window 344857 -> 349979 ms is `sent` +350 / `fold` +284 = ~66 installs for ~350 reports (**5.3 : 1, ~13 installs/s**) against the pre-storm control `sent` +26 / `fold` +0 = **1 : 1** at 320755 -> 326403 ms. · SDHCWRITE (branch exec-rmbp-sdhcwrite, parent a38ad49d; the branch tip is the sha — the seat fills it at the fold) — **MILESTONE 4b's DRIVER HALF IS IN: CMD25 WRITE_MULTIPLE_BLOCK on PIO, with CMD12 STOP_TRANSMISSION as the controller's Auto CMD12 and the programming-busy bound taken from the SPEC's WRITE figure instead of the READ one. THE POSTURE HALF THE FLIGHT-11 SCREENSHOT NEEDS IS BLOCKED, and the block is a one-line change in a file this arc may not touch.** `sdhc::write_blocks_512` mirrors the CMD18 read path step for step (Multi/Single Block Select + Block Count Enable + Auto CMD12, `TM_DIR_READ` OMITTED — the ABSENCE of the direction bit is the write), re-reads the write-protect PIN and the card's CSD at every call, and closes the CARD's side with `abort_data_transfer` on every failure. The correction that is engineering and not transcription: Physical Layer §4.6.2.2 states the WRITE timeout as a PER-BLOCK 250 ms, while `DATA_TIMEOUT_MS` is 200 ms and is a READ bound — a CMD25 path copying CMD18's waits would convict a legal card the first time it went busy on a full write buffer, so every wait card programming can hold off now scales as `PROG_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS * count`. The fixture writes the FOUR blocks BELOW 4a's own scratch sector, re-proves each of them blank AND outside every partition extent (a window is only as safe as its least safe sector), stamps each block with its OWN LBA so a wrong-stride CMD25 names the block, verifies by CMD18 read-back, restores and verifies the restore — and it runs ONLY after 4a's single-block ladder reached `-> PASS`, which is what makes a bad compare indict CMD25 rather than the card or the read path. **WIRE, under `UNAOS_SDW=1`:** `[sdhc] write n=4 lba=<num_blocks-5> verify=ok busy_ms=<n>` then `:: sdhc: w2 armed=1 ... restore=IDENTICAL reason=none -> PASS ::`. Without the knob: neither line, and no CMD25 or CMD12 word in the image at all (4a's property, extended). **STOPPED, and reported rather than done (EXECUTOR-BRIEF §3).** The brief's items (2)-posture and (3) — `sdhc=rw` and a writable FAT mount for PRTSCR-VOL rung 1 — cannot be built from `drivers/`, `fs/vfs.rs` or `video/prtscr.rs`. The posture has ONE definition, `fat::BlockSource::write_veto` (`fs/fat.rs:725`), whose `Sdhc` arm returns `Some(...)` unconditionally; `FatFs::write_veto`, `FatBackend::read_only`, the `HOMESOIL: posture` census and `prtscr::mount_capture_target` rung 1 are all forwards to it, and `fs/vfs.rs` has NO `Sdhc` mount arm (its only mention is the doc line at :1106, which is what the brief read as one). The fix is one line in that one place and its shape is already precedented beside it — the `TegraSd` arm is `if crate::drivers::block::tegra_sd_writes_admitted() { None } else { Some(TEGRA_SD_VETO) }`. The `UNAOS_SDW_RW=1` knob is a cargo feature, hence a FOUR-place wiring (`crates/kernel/Cargo.toml`, arroyo map, `builder/src/main.rs`, `arm_features`), two of which are also outside the brief. **NEXT CUT MUST CARRY:** `fs/fat.rs`, `fs/sdhc4c.rs`, `crates/kernel/Cargo.toml`, `builder/src/main.rs` — and Peter's decision first, because the mechanism serves both answers: does the boot volume's DEFAULT posture become `rw` (the card IS the hard drive on this bench), or stay `ro` with the reserved extent widened to a real Pictures/ path? `block::write_blocks_sdhc` is deliberately UNTOUCHED and still loops CMD24: `fs::sdhc4c` publishes `cmd24 == bytes / 512` as a falsifiable prediction, so routing it through CMD25 would make a shipped witness name a command the card never saw. rmbp-ledger B140; `docs/dev/OS/07_USB_STORAGE/sdhc.md` §14 (§14.7 is the owed list). Gates: `./arroyo check` rc=0; `knoboff sdw` and `knoboff sdhcblk` exit 0 `warm=yes` (the whole arc is APPENDED at `sdhc.rs`'s tail and the one call site is a LINE-NEUTRAL append — `pub fn probe()` is at :3448 in both a38ad49d and this tree).
· SPLASHGATE (branch exec-rmbp-splashgate, parent 4ab15184; the branch tip is the sha — the seat fills it at the fold)  **CRYSTALBOOT'S OWED CLAUSE IS PAID: `./arroyo test-splash` BOOTS THE SHIPPED POLARITY, SO THE BOOT SPLASH IS ON A WIRE FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS REPO'S HISTORY.** Ledger row B149 carries the counts and both replays. The defect CRYSTALBOOT named is a GATE defect and it was exact: `./arroyo test` force-arms `UNAOS_WITNESS=1` (`unaos/arroyo`, the `case` at the head of the file, and `${UNAOS_WITNESS:-1}` means an EMPTY value does not disarm it either), while `witness` compiles the `boot_splash` CALL out of `main.rs:225-234` — so no battery run this repo has ever made had a splash on its panel, flight 11's own image had none, and SPLASH-1 / SPLASH-2 / SPLASH-ALIVE / SPLASH-SEAMLESS / SPLASHRETIRE were five arcs no run had executed. **THE FIX IS A VERB THE ARMING CASE DOES NOT NAME**, which is the only shape that can work: `test_splash` re-execs with `UNAOS_SPLASHLANE=1 UNAOS_WC=1 UNAOS_QEMU_FULL=1`, `witness`/`usbdebug`/`bootlog` stay OFF, and the boot's own banner says so (`⚡ kernel features: ehcihid,kbdwit,sdhcblk,smolnet,wc,sdwrite`). It replays the NEW `unaos/scripts/specs/x86-splash.spec` (two REQUIREs, two FORBIDs, its own COMPLETE marker — the lane cannot borrow x86-test.spec's zeolite marker, which is battery-only and absent by construction here) and then certifies BOTH splash strings on the ELF the builder left behind, with a present- and an absent-control. **WHAT IT DOES NOT CLOSE, said here so nobody reads it as more than it is:** the fold's SEAM arm (`advance` asking `fbcon::panel_console_live()`) is METAL-ONLY — QEMU has no Kepler, `panel_console_resume` has zero hits on this lane's capture, and the retirement is stamped by the `gui` BACKSTOP. So the gate proves the splash is painted, retired ONCE, at a NAMED site, on the build a person boots; the seam itself still wants a flight. **AND THE CARD-LINE DECISION THIS SURFACES, WHICH IS NOT MINE TO MAKE AND IS NAMED RATHER THAN TAKEN:** the flight images are cut WITH `witness`+`usbdebug` because that is what the fixtures need, and those two are exactly what compile the splash away — so the splash Peter sees on the bench is ABSENT BY CONSTRUCTION from every image this project has flown. Either a flight flies the shipped polarity (no fixtures on that boot, by definition), or the splash stays a thing only QEMU and a hand-cut card can show. Gate doc: `docs/dev/STRUCTURAL_GATES.md` §GATE-SPLASH.
· SDHCPOST (branch exec-rmbp-sdhcpost, parent 60eda93a; the branch tip is the sha — the seat fills it at the fold) — **THE CARD IS WRITABLE, UNDER A KNOB, AND THE POSTURE HAS ONE DEFINITION AT LAST.** Flight 11's `:: PRTSCR: REFUSED READ-ONLY (source=sdhc … SDHC-4c)` is closed: `sdhc=rw` iff the `sdw-rw` feature is built AND the write-protect PIN read AT MOUNT says enabled AND the block layer has a live write path, else the same refusal as today with the reason naming which of the three said no (`sdw-off`/`wp-protected`/`no-write-path`), on a truth table printed once at the mount. **B140 SAID THE POSTURE HAD ONE READER AND IT HAD TWO** — `block::handle_write_veto` carried a second copy and `sdwrite_posture_selftest` leg 8 compares the two for every source in `fat::ALL_SOURCES`, so the prescribed one-line lift would have printed `sdhc=rw!DISAGREES` + `FAIL ::` and reddened every QEMU run. Fixed by leaving ONE arm: `drivers::block::sdhc_write_veto` (§SDHCPOST at that file's tail), both readers forward to it, and it takes the caller's own refusal string so the knob-off twin is `#[inline(always)] fn(s) -> Some(s)`. **CMD25 IS ROUTED and the number is the reason:** `prtscr` hands 32 KiB per `write_grow` → `write_span` → `fat::write_sectors`, which chunks at `MAX_BLOCKS_PER_OP`=64, and `sdhc::MB_MAX_BLOCKS` is 64 — one capture slice is EXACTLY one CMD25, so the rMBP's 15.5 MB screenshot is ~484 multi-block writes against ~31,700 single-block ones, **~64x fewer card transactions**; the ROUTE IS GATED ON `sdw-rw`, NOT `sdw`: a `ro` build keeps the CMD24 loop and prints `cmd24=` verbatim (every capture since 4c is read against it), and only an `sdw-rw` build takes CMD25 and prints `sectors=`, whose prediction `sectors == bytes / 512` is true of both commands — so neither polarity's witness lies and `knoboff sdw` stays byte-identical, which a rename on the shipped line would have broken (measured: the first attempt came back `58132 byte(s) differ`). **SDHC-4c's closure claims are RETIRED while the knob is on, not strained:** a file verb on a writable volume IS a FAT mutator, so `fs/sdhc4c.rs`'s module doc says “true with `sdw-rw` off, retired with it on”, the permit bypass says so on the wire once, and an expected mutation is counted apart from the `!!` defect line. Widening the extent instead is structurally impossible — a capture's FAT and directory sectors live BELOW `data_start`, out of reach of any extent. **ALSO UNBLOCKED: R54.** PRTSCR's no-session refusal is checked FIRST, ahead of the PRTSCR-VOL ladder, so without a session no posture can land a capture; arroyo's `UNAOS_LOGIN` note claimed the x86 builder had no mapping — STALE, it maps both `UNAOS_LOGIN` and `UNAOS_LOGINST` — and the note is corrected in place. **WIRE, armed (`UNAOS_SDHCBLK=1 UNAOS_SDW=1 UNAOS_SDW_RW=1 UNAOS_WC=1 UNAOS_LOGIN=1 UNAOS_LOGINST=1`):** `:: SDHCPOST: posture sdw-rw=1 wp-pin=enabled write-path=live -> sdhc=rw reason=none … ::`, `HOMESOIL: posture … sdhc=rw`, `:: PRTSCR-VOL: rung=1 … ::`. Without the knob: every one of those reads `ro` and the refusal is verbatim today's. **⚠ THE DEFAULT IS UNCHANGED HERE, AND PETER HAS NOW RULED IT: R59 (2026-09-22), “read write.”** rw by default on every board, WP pin plus a named opt-out the only refusals, SDHC-4c's permit ladder and `UNAOS_SDW_RW` retiring. This branch carries the MECHANISM R59 needs and NOT the flip, because the flip is a re-cut and not an edit: the feature inverts name and polarity in all five wiring places, the knoboff claim inverts with it (“knob-off equals baseline” becomes “the opt-out image equals baseline”), CMD25 and `sectors=` move into the default image, the permit ladder retires rather than being bypassed, and “every board” reaches `TegraSd` and the Pi. rmbp-ledger B155 carries the costs of both answers. rmbp-ledger B140 (appended) and B155 (new); `docs/dev/OS/07_USB_STORAGE/sdhc.md` §15, §14.7 amended. Gates in the commit body: `./arroyo check` rc=0 (the `x86-all` KERNEL_CFG_MATRIX leg names `sdw-rw`, `x86-all-nowitness` does not — both polarities type-checked; knob→builder wiring OK; a `scripts/k8-reach.registry` row records the knob as x86-only with its `--evidence` count, the FIFTH wiring place a new knob needs and one the four-place shape does not name); `knoboff sdw` and `knoboff sdw-rw` exit 0 `warm=yes`; two QEMU runs rc=0 COMPLETE; go-red by mutating the pin read; artifact `sdhc=rw` 1 armed / 0 plain.

New file `drivers/uvc.rs` (USB Video Class census + `VS_PROBE_CONTROL` negotiation, CONTROL TRANSFERS ONLY: no isochronous pipe, no `VS_COMMIT_CONTROL`, no `SET_CUR` on any VideoControl control), reached by ONE line-neutral folded line in `configure_hid`. Doc: `docs/dev/OS/07_USB_STORAGE/uvc.md`. **THE QEMU HALF IS FLOWN AND GREEN:** `UNAOS_UVC=1 UNAOS_WC=1 ./arroyo test 240` rc=0 prints `:: uvc: selftest fixture=289B formats=2 frames=3 alts=2 probe_roundtrip=ok probe_reply=ok -> PASS ::` and `[uvc] skip addr=1 reason=no-video-iad` (QEMU's EHCI keyboard, declined at one line and zero transfers), with go-red on the same command rc=1 `got=57346 want=640` from a one-character parser mutation. The knob is wired in all four places a knob needs — `arroyo`, `builder/src/main.rs` (the x86 list the BOOTED kernel is built from, for media verbs AND for `./arroyo test`; a knob in arroyo alone is the sdwrite/rastmc failure and it was measured on this arc as `feature=uvc … hits=0 -> MISSING`), the `x86-all` and `x86-all-nowitness` legs, and the `banner-cert.sh` row — plus a `k8-reach.registry` NA row. **SO THE FLIGHT IS THE CAMERA AND ONLY THE CAMERA:** ONE ORDINARY BOOT with `UNAOS_UVC=1` on the line. QEMU models no UVC device, so `print_census`, the full descriptor re-read and every `VS_PROBE_CONTROL` transfer have still never executed anywhere. **WATCH-LIST, first number first:** `wTotalLength=` on `[uvc] candidate` — the EP0 data buffer is 256 B and USB has no offset on GET_DESCRIPTOR, so over 256 the census is VideoControl-only (it says so) and the next rung's first job is a bigger buffer, not an isochronous pipe. Then `[uvc] vs fmt=`/`[uvc] frame` (the formats and geometries this panel actually offers), `[uvc] alt=… mps=… mult=…` (the menu the next rung picks from) and `[uvc] probe stage=cur … dwMaxPayloadTransferSize=` with its `agreed=` verdict (the requirement that menu has to cover).

Two mechanisms: the drain and the present are ONE TASK ON ONE CORE (the storm window dispatches at **0.4-2.7/s** against 10-50 reports/s; `presents=4727` over ~998 s is a ~211 ms mean loop period), and `x86_ptr_install` opened with a BLOCKING `*WRITER.lock()` on the input band — B1's boot-8 shape, reintroduced by B117 two weeks after B1 removed it one function over. The install is also the sole drain of `pal::EVENT_QUEUE`, so the stall and the fold storm share one cause.     [ptrinstall] installs=<n> reports=<n> lag_max_ms=<n> coalesced=<n> drains=<n> folds=<n> fold_age_max_ms=<n> drain_gap_max_ms=<n> panel_busy=<n>

  READ IT LIKE THIS, and each reading is falsifiable in both directions. `installs == reports` must STILL hold — B117's identity is untouched, and a break there convicts this arc rather than the storm. `fold_age_max_ms` should sit CLOSE TO `lag_max_ms`, because nearly every storm dispatch is a fold; if it is much LOWER then the long waits are NOT the folded population and "jumping" has another home. `drain_gap_max_ms` is the new number and is expected in the HUNDREDS of ms against that ~211 ms mean — it is the compositor's stall seen from the pointer, and it is the term to hand VUGPERF. `panel_busy` is this fix's own witness: **> 0 proves the installer met the contention it used to BLOCK on and absorbed it**; `panel_busy=0` means this flight never met it, and is read with `drain_gap_max_ms` — a busy render core with `panel_busy=0` would be the surprise worth chasing. `[inwedge] refused` now includes this population by design; subtract `panel_busy` for the pre-B134 reading. Expect also one `:: PTRLAG: … :: PASS ::` beside the input task's dispatch line. **STILL OWED AFTER THIS ARC, and it is the bigger half:** the arrow's PIXELS still wait on the compositor — `claim_bounded(2 ms)` spins on the input band (flight 11 `[wedge9] refused=61127 masked=54070 retried=84 serviced=7153`: ~7,057 unmasked refusals against 84 useful retries, **~1.2%**, i.e. ~14 s of input-core spin that ends in `owe_repaint` regardless), and an owed repaint is cashed only by `wm::composite` under the `REPAIR_MIN_MS` floor — the storm's own cadence. Line: `UNAOS_HDA=1 UNAOS_HDATONE=1 ./arroyo esp-x86` (plus the flight's usual knobs). WATCH-LIST, in wire order, and every one of these is a line flight 11 did NOT have. **(a)** `[hda] pair member=1/2 pin=0x0b dac=0x04 dev=speaker assoc=1 seq=0 chan=0 stereo=1 primary=1` then `member=2/2 pin=0x0a dac=0x03 … seq=2 chan=0 stereo=1 primary=0` — **the pair, ordered by sequence; if `np`/`member=x/2` reads `1/1` the association scan found no second member and THAT is the finding.** **(b)** `[hda] bdl entry=0/1 addr=0x… len=96000 ioc=1 flags=0x00000001 (readback)` twice — `ioc=1` on BOTH; an `ioc=0` explains flight 11's `bcis=0` outright and ends that question. **(c)** `[hda] gpio afg=0x01 caps=0x…. gpios=N gpos=N gpis=N unsol=. wake=. mask=0x.. enable=0x..->0x.. dir=0x..->0x.. data=0x..->0x.. stuck=0 -> set` — **THE ONE LINE THIS FLIGHT EXISTS FOR.** `gpios=` is READ, not assumed (the CS4206's GPIO count is unknown to this tree); `stuck=1` means the codec refused the drive and is a different answer from "driven and still silent". **(d)** `[hda] power member=0 dac=0x04 set=D0 actual=D0 raw=0x00 pin=0x0b set=D0 actual=D0 raw=0x00 entry_dac=0x.. entry_pin=0x..` per member — a non-zero `actual=` nibble is a node that did NOT reach D0 and is then the cause. **(e)** `[hda] amp member=0 dac=0x04 gain=0x…. mute=0 steps=.. pin=0x0b gain=0x…. mute=0 out_amp=. pinctl=0x40 out_en=1 hp_en=0 fmt_conv=0x0011 fmt_want=0x0011 fmt_match=1` per member — `mute=1`, `out_en=0` or `fmt_match=0` each name their own defect. **(f)** `[hda] bind member=. dac=0x.. want_tag=1 want_chan=0 bound=0x10 tag=1 chan=0 ok=1` per member. **(g)** the tone line, now counting: `[hda] tone stream=0 lpib=0 -> N (max 192000) bcis=N … wraps=1 consumed=230000ish rate_bps=~191997 expect_bps=192000 members=2 ctl_running=0x001….` and `:: HDA-TONE: lpib_advanced=1 walked=1 wraps=1 bcis=N tag_ok=1 … members=2 -> PASS ::`. **THE SCORE IS THE EAR, and the wire verdict is deliberately ear-free so a PASS with silence is still a REAL finding.** If the room is silent with `gpio … -> set` and every readback green, the GPIO SET is wrong rather than the GPIO idea, and the next executor narrows WITHIN the set (one GPIO per boot, or the complement) — that is a boot-per-bit job and it is cheap. ⚠ `bcis=0` may well repeat and **it no longer fails the run**: the verdict's second witness is `walked = wraps>0 || bcis>0`. The open question it leaves is whether `INTCTL.SIE` gates the `SDnSTS.BCIS` latch on this PCH the way `RIRBCTL.RINTCTL` gates `RIRBSTS.RINTFL` (hda.md §2.4, §6) — a one-knob flight of its own, and it changes the `wrote-intctl=0(audited)` invariant, so it is NOT folded into this boot. Gates already run on the branch: `./arroyo check` rc=0 both arches, the `UNAOS_WC=1 UNAOS_HDA=1 UNAOS_HDATONE=1 UNAOS_QEMU_FULL=1 ./arroyo test 120` suite with `hda-duplex` still PASS, one go-red, `knoboff hda` / `knoboff hda-tone` exit 0; docs/dev/OS/11_AUDIO/hda.md §6, rmbp-ledger B130
· NEW  QUARRYFONT FLIGHT  **the glass is the gate, because the defect is one Peter saw with his eyes.** Knob line: the ordinary desktop line plus `UNAOS_QUARRY=1` (A10's knob half is still open — without it there is no Quarry to look at). OPEN QUARRY FROM THE DOCK TILE and look INSIDE the window. WHAT THE GLASS SHOULD SHOW: the tree rows, the NAME/SIZE/MODIFIED header and the path bar in the SAME typeface as the menu bar and the dock captions — smooth-edged, not the stepped 2x-replicated blocks of flights 8-10. The panel is 2880x1800, so the predicate picks `Face::Chrome`: 9x20 cells, ~14 characters per 128 px. WATCH-LIST LINE, printed once when the window opens and the only wire evidence this arc has on metal: `[quarry] open win=<n> surf=<w>x<h> ts=2 box=<w>x<h> at (<x>,<y>) volumes=<n> tree-rows=<n> list-rows=<n> cwd=/ face=noto20-aa cell=9x20` — `face=noto20-aa` is the claim; `face=noto16-aa` would mean the panel predicate read the panel wrong, and no `face=` field at all would mean the card carries a pre-QUARRYFONT image. A SECOND THING TO TRY IN THE SAME SITTING, free: click a tree row and a list row (QUARRYCLICK, same branch). The wire owes `[quarry] press at (<sx>,<sy>) row=<n> kind=<dir|file|none> -> <cd|select|launch|miss>` and a `[clickroute] … -> quarry` beside it; flight 10's `-> consume deliver=0` on an in-window press is the regression this would catch.
· NEW  QUARRYOPEN FLIGHT  **open a FILE, not just a folder — and this time the wire will say what happened either way.** Branch `exec-rmbp-quarryopen`, parent `b9edcfa1`; rmbp-ledger **A10 (5)** and **B136**; `docs/dev/OS/05_USER_EXPERIENCE/quarry.md` §6.4. Knob line: the ordinary desktop line plus `UNAOS_QUARRY=1`, and add `UNAOS_FACET=1` if the card carries a `.PNG` worth double-pressing (without it the viewer is not compiled and a PNG is correctly handler-less). WHY: flight 11's *"could not open a file"* was NOT a broken opener — the capture carries no double-press at all (two presses 6517 ms apart with a TREE press between them, which re-stamps the pane and breaks the pair on its own). WHAT TO DO AT THE GLASS, in one sitting: (1) press a tree row ON THE TRIANGLE and again ON THE NAME — flight 11 only ever hit the triangle (x = 18..26 is inside the marker box at `ts = 2`), which is why every dir press read `select` and none read `cd`; (2) **double-press a `.ELF` under `/apps` quickly** — two presses inside 500 ms, no other press between them and not in the other pane; (3) double-press a `.TXT`; (4) select a file row and press **Enter**. WATCH-LIST LINES, and what each one means. `[quarry] press at (<sx>,<sy>) row=<n> kind=file -> open kind=elf handler=launch` immediately followed by `[quarry] launch … pid=<n> asid=<n>` / `:: QUARRY-LAUNCH: …` — the gesture and its effect, paired. `[quarry] press at (…) row=<n> kind=file -> select (no handler for .TXT)` — heard and refused, with `[quarry] open UNHANDLED path=… kind=text handler=none — no handler for .TXT (…)` beside it. `[quarry] enter row=<n> kind=file -> open kind=elf handler=launch` — the keyboard saying exactly what the pointer said. `[quarry] press at (…) row=<n> kind=dir -> cd` on a tree press that lands on the NAME (a press on the triangle still says `select`, and that is correct). A `.PNG` reads `-> open kind=png handler=facet` with `UNAOS_FACET=1` and `-> select (no handler for .PNG)` without it. THE REGRESSIONS THIS CATCHES: a `-> miss` on the second press of a double-press is the pre-QUARRYOPEN wire back (the model diff swallowing a gesture the window answered); two `-> select` lines on one row with nothing between them and under 500 ms apart means `is_double` is not firing and B136's drain jitter is wider than 500 ms on this board — quote the `:: PTR: … (down edge)` stamps and the `[ptrinstall] … lag_max_ms=` beside them, because that pair is the only evidence that decides it. FREE IN THE SAME SITTING: `:: QUARRYOPEN: dbl=500ms clock=live … :: PASS ::` lands in the boot battery long before the glass is touched; a `FAIL` there means the arithmetic went before the hand did.
· NEW  BOOTCLOCK FLIGHT  **the A12 number, and it is ONE line on an ordinary boot — no knob, no extra sitting.** WATCH-LIST LINE (read it before `BPACE`, it lands right after `:: apic: … calibrated`): `:: BOOTCLOCK: firmware->loader=<ms> loader-read=<ms> loader-jump=<ms> kernel-entry=<tsc> tsc_hz=<n>(measured) ::`. HOW TO READ IT. `firmware->loader` is an ABSOLUTE stamp — `rdtsc` from the last processor reset — so it is firmware POST **plus Peter's hand at the ⌥ picker**, an upper bound and never a firmware timing; its value is that it is the first REPEATABLE number for that phase, so the comparison that answers A12 is TWO flights of the SAME media, not one flight against a feeling. `loader-read` is the ELF read alone and is the term A12 nominates (it grows with the image every arc). `loader-jump` is load + relocation + ACPI/EDID/volume discovery + `exit_boot_services`. `kernel-entry` is raw cycles at the `BPACE entry` stamp, so `kernel-entry − tsc_loader_jump` is the hand-off gap and `kernel-entry / tsc_hz` is the whole pre-kernel phase — cross-check it against the squawk-mark bound (≤ 47.7 s F8 / ≤ 49.8 s F9) and against `BPACE total gui=`, which it must precede. EXPECTED ORDER OF MAGNITUDE: tens of seconds, versus tens-to-hundreds of MILLISECONDS in QEMU; if the bench prints milliseconds the stamps are not the ones the boot took. THE TWO NULL READINGS, both pre-registered: `firmware->loader=absent loader-read=absent loader-jump=absent` means the card carries a `bootloader.efi` built before this field (re-pack the whole ESP — the loader and the kernel are one ABI and old media silently predates it), and `tsc_hz=0(uncalibrated)` with `cy` suffixes means the ACPI PM timer never calibrated. Neither is a fabricated number, and `kernel-entry=` prints in both.
· NEW  PARTINSTALL METAL — **the first install into a Disk-Utility partition on Peter's internal SSD. ATTENDED, DESTRUCTIVE, a FRESH GO every time — and the operator now has a brief: `docs/dev/OS/10_INSTALL/SITTING-1.md` (INSTALLSIT, 2026-09-16). That file is what the seat follows; this row is the state.** RE-MEASURED at 11ca67f1 with AHCIWRITE 504a4634, INSTALLVERB fb29c268 and SELFGUARD-AHCI a84c8f32 all folded. **(a) THE OLD BOOT-1/BOOT-2 SPLIT IN THIS ROW WAS WRONG AND IS STRUCK:** `UNAOS_AHCI=1` alone censuses NOTHING on the SSD — `install/mod.rs:208`'s `Ahci` READ arm is `Err(NotReady)` under `all(ahci, not(ahci-write))` and opens ONLY under `ahci-write`, so a read-only boot needs `UNAOS_AHCI_WRITE=1` too, and without it the verb prints `ahci0: present, NOT censused` (`shell.rs:8134`) and `:: PINSTALL: census part=…` never appears for the SSD. ONE image serves both boots; what separates them is what Peter TYPES, not what is compiled. **(b) ⚠ THE HAZARD THAT KNOB PAIR CREATES:** `installdemo` + `ahci-write` with NO `instgui` WRITES BY ITSELF at boot — `main.rs:1817`, `main.rs:6048` → `install/mod.rs:413` → `partition.rs:1021` `sata_fixture()`, which takes any SATA disk whose GPT parses (`:1346`), names the first slot the ladder clears (`:1376`), mints a grant (`:1438`) and writes (`:1470`). `sata_is_boot_device` (`:621`) will read `install-self=eligible` on port 0, because the bench boot volume is the SD card (`root=sdhc:/kernel.elf … by=content … -> PASS`, `docs/dev/evidence/rmbp-0916/flight8-9/FLIGHT8-9.md`), not the SSD. `UNAOS_INSTGUI=1` cfg-erases both call sites and is a SAFETY knob on this line, not a cosmetic one. **(c) ⛔ STOP — THE VOLUME IS NOT BOOTABLE, and this decides what the sitting may claim:** `install_into_partition` (`partition.rs:944`) builds its payload from `demo_tree()` at `:952`, unconditionally — four SYNTHETIC files, `EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI` = 12,288 B of `body("UNAOS-PARTINSTALL-LOADER\n", …)` (`:824`), not a PE image and not this kernel — and the real-tree reader `install/clone.rs:80 snapshot()` has NO caller anywhere in `unaos/crates/kernel/src`. So boot 2's verdict is *“the write landed inside the partition and every neighbour is byte-identical”*, never *“it boots”*; the ⌥ picker is worth LOOKING at (does it list a non-ESP FAT volume at all — still metal-UNPROVEN) but the volume must NOT be started. One call site retires this; NOT taken here. **(d) WHAT THE WRITE PATH REFUSES ON PETER'S SSD BY CONSTRUCTION TODAY:** the disk-wide `InstallTarget`'s `Ahci` write arm is `Err(NotReady)` in EVERY cfg, `ahci-write` included (`install/mod.rs:232`), so nothing can name LBA 0 or either GPT header; the only SATA write is `PartitionTarget` carrying a `WriteGrant`, minted in exactly one place (`partition.rs:585`); three bounds in three files — `partition.rs:748` (`map`, `BadLba`, never a clamp), `drivers/block.rs:3214` (`write_sectors_granted`, with the `[ahci] write REFUSED lba=… outside grant` witness), `drivers/ahci.rs:975` (`write_block_at`, device capacity); and the sitting passes no `--as-esp`, so the GPT is never written and a power loss cannot corrupt the table. **(e) THE SLOT PETER MUST MAKE, measured:** Disk Utility's *MS-DOS (FAT)* volume is REFUSED `reason=partition-not-empty content=FAT` — `Content::is_installable` matches ONLY `Empty` (`partition.rs:121`) — and there is NO `--reformat` grant (`install_into_partition`'s only switch is `as_esp`, `:944`; the verb's only third word is `--as-esp`, `shell.rs:8374`). So: create it as MS-DOS (FAT) **for its Microsoft Basic Data type GUID**, which is deliberately absent from `FOREIGN_TYPE_GUIDS` (`partition.rs:198`), THEN zero its first 8 sectors from macOS (`PROBE_BYTES = 8 * SECTOR`, `partition.rs:62`) so the probe reads `content=empty`. Size floor measured: `size_requirement` = 65525×512 + 61952 + 1 MiB = **34,659,328 B ≈ 33.1 MiB** (`partition.rs:494`); ≥ 64 GiB for a real system. **(f) THE KNOB LINE (image 3):** flight 8's line (`~/unaos-bench/flash/rmbp/MANIFEST:639`) minus the four probe knobs, plus `UNAOS_AHCI_WRITE=1 UNAOS_INSTALLDEMO=1 UNAOS_INSTGUI=1 UNAOS_QUARRY=1`; `UNAOS_WC=1` is already there and is REQUIRED by `video/mod.rs:159`. Four-place checked — map `arroyo:2325`/`:2356`/`:616`/`:993`/`:1524`, builder `main.rs:283`/`:294`/`:647`/`:610`/`:122`, `ahci-write` stripped from aarch64 at `arroyo:2551` (and `ahci` at `:2539`) and present on `x86-all` at `:4427`, so **no Pi and no Jetson media hash moves**. Before the card: `LC_ALL=C grep -a -o -F 'WRITE-DMA-EXT-0x35' kernel.elf` must be ≥ 1, on the ARTIFACT. **(g) NOT MEASURED, and it is the sitting's first act:** the SSD's slot list. `awk 'index($0,"PINSTALL")' ~/unaos-bench/capture/rmbp12-flight8/ttyUSB0.log` = **0 lines** (so does INSTALLVERB); the whole of what the wire has ever said about that disk is `:: AHCI: port=0 model="APPLE SSD SM768E" sectors=1467339812 lba48=1 ::`, `:: AHCI: port=0 sector0 sig=0xaa55 kind=GPT ::` and `[bootdisk] volume source=ahci port=0 vol=EFI serial=0x67e317ed blocks=1467339812 volumes_by_content=2 ::`. And there is **no `:: PART: gpt …` line anywhere in this tree** — `:: PART:` is MBR/unafs/fat only (`drivers/block.rs:2292-2324`, `fs/unafs.rs`, `fs/fat.rs:1768`); the GPT view is `:: PINSTALL: census part=…` (`partition.rs:314`). Ledger B89 / B91
· ✓ FLOWN  WIFI4 FLIGHT  **FLOWN 2026-09-22 (flight 11) and it read EXACTLY as this row pre-registered: `:: wifi4: REFUSED reason=wifi3-upload-not-proven ::` then `:: wifi4: end ok=0 stage=gate radio-id=0x00000000 wrote-radio-addr=0(audited) wrote-radio-data=0 wrote-core-regs=0 wrote-wrapper=0 restore=N/A elapsed=0ms ::` — the wifi3 upload refused above it, so the rung stayed inert and wrote nothing. NOT A DEFECT, and the latch worked: this row said that reading was expected on any boot where the upload does not complete. The §S5(a)/(b) predicates are therefore STILL UNMEASURED — they ride the next armed boot (WIFISHM FLIGHT, below), and the wire shape below is unchanged and still what to score it on.** **the §S5 rung is built and inert; it rides the wifi3 boot and costs nothing if that boot refuses.** Branch `exec-rmbp-wifi4`, ledger B124, `docs/dev/OS/06_NETWORK_STACK/bcm4331.md` §S5's IMPLEMENTED block. **KNOB:** add `UNAOS_WIFI4=1` to whatever flight line the STATE row names for the wifi3 boot — it is NOT a line of its own and must never be flown without `UNAOS_WIFI3=1`, because `wifi4` is gated on `wifi3`'s verdict and alone it can only refuse. Four-place checked: Cargo `wifi4 = ["wifi3"]`, `arroyo` map + `arm_features` strip + `x86-all` matrix leg, `builder/src/main.rs` — **no Pi and no Jetson media hash moves.** Before the card: `LC_ALL=C grep -a -o -F ':: wifi4: ' kernel.elf` must be ≥ 1, on the ARTIFACT. **SEQUENCING — it is INERT until wifi3 succeeds on the SAME boot.** The gate is latched at `upload_verdict()`, the single funnel every exit of `upload_ucode` passes through, so the latch and the wire line are one event: no `-> UPLOADED` this boot (upload failed, or a gate above it returned first) ⇒ `:: wifi4: REFUSED reason=wifi3-upload-not-proven ::` and NOTHING is written. That is the expected reading on any boot where the destructive upload does not complete, and it is not a defect. **WIRE SHAPE ON A SUCCESSFUL BOOT** (after `:: wifi2: ucode upload … -> UPLOADED ::`): `:: wifi4: begin — … WRITES: d11+0x03f6 ONLY …`, then `radio-addr pre-image d11+0x03f6=<w>`, `radio-select 1/2 … wrote=0x0001 readback=0x0001 took=1`, `radio-select 2/2 …`, `RESTORE d11+0x03f6 <- pre-image <w> … restored=MATCH`, `radio-id raw=0x<32> hi=… lo=… rev=<r> ver=0x<4> mfg=0x17f expected-mfg=0x17f MATCH`, `radio-id cross-check order-agree=1 … addr-echo=1 … dark=0`, `radio-id verdict=VALID gated-on=mfg-is-0x17F+addr-echo+order-agree+not-dark`, `radio-ver 0x<4> vs 0x2059 MATCH|DIFFERS (ADVISORY … NOT GATED …)`, `phy-alive raw=0x9701 … type=7 expected=7 … psm-run=1 want=1 verdict=PHY-ALIVE`, then three refusals by name (`phy-reset REFUSED reason=ai-wrapper-phy-reset-bit-UNPINNED`, `channel-tune REFUSED reason=htphy-2059-tables-UNPINNED`, `rx-chain NOT ATTEMPTED`), then `:: wifi4: end ok=1 stage=phy radio-id=0x<32> wrote-radio-addr=3(audited) wrote-radio-data=0 wrote-core-regs=0 wrote-wrapper=0 restore=MATCH elapsed=…`. **WHAT THE FLIGHT BUYS AND WHAT IT DOES NOT.** It buys §S5(a) — the radio answers with a Broadcom manufacturer field through ports both spec generations pin — and §S5(b)'s read-back predicate, that the PHY still decodes to type 7 with the core out of reset after wifi3's destructive reset. It does **not** buy "it is a 2059": neither spec generation's radio-version table carries 0x2059 or any HT-PHY radio, so the version is printed ADVISORY and gated on nothing, exactly as §S4-W5 residual 4 treats the ucode revision number. **⚠ THE ONE RESIDUAL, on the wire not in a comment:** the write is to a shared address latch and the PSM is running by construction; a lost race gives us a garbage word (caught by `addr-echo` and `order-agree`) and the PSM at most ONE mis-addressed radio access. `addr-echo=0` or `order-agree=0` on the capture means exactly that and the id must not be decoded from it.
· NEW  WIFISHM FLIGHT  **the SECOND armed `UNAOS_WIFI3=1` boot — the one where the upload actually runs, and it is DESTRUCTIVE with no unwind.** Branch `exec-rmbp-wifishm`, ledger **B131**, `docs/dev/OS/06_NETWORK_STACK/bcm4331.md` §S4's flight-11 subsection. **WHY THERE IS A SECOND BOOT:** flight 11 refused at `upload REFUSED reason=macctl-shape … shm-enabled=0 want=1`, and that `want=1` was WRONG — `[SPEC-V3 ChipInit]` step 1 writes `MACCTL <- 0x404` and step 4 writes `0x20402`, and `[SPEC-V4 802.11/Registers]` decodes BOTH with `SHM Enabled` (0x100) CLEAR, so the spec streams the microcode through the SHM window with the bit clear by construction and the rung was refusing its own spec three writes before clearing the bit itself. The term is removed from the gate and REPORTED instead; `BE` stays gated and alone. **KNOB: unchanged** — the same flight line as flight 11 (`UNAOS_WIFI3=1 UNAOS_WIFI4=1` plus the flight's usual knobs). No new feature, no new knob, no media-hash move on Pi or Jetson. **READ THIS FIRST, ahead of the gate — the new read-only line:** `:: wifi2: shm-probe routing=0x0001(Shared Memory, …) ctl-wrote=0x00010000 ctl-readback=0x… shared[+0x00]=0x…. macctl=0x… shm-enabled=0 shm-upper=0 big-endian=0 window-answers=YES|NO(…) ::`. It writes `SHM_CONTROL` ONLY (the read path's address-window selector) and is the FIRST shared-memory read this tree has ever taken on this silicon — §S4a was written to take one and never flew, and two sentences in bcm4331.md called that never-executed code 'metal-proven' until now. **`window-answers=YES` beside `shm-enabled=0` is direct metal proof that host SHM access does not need the bit**; a `NO` is not a blocker (the spec argument stands alone) but it is the finding of the boot and must be quoted. **THEN, on this boot, there must be NO `macctl-*` refusal at all** (flight 11 measured `big-endian=0`), and the rung proceeds: `:: wifi2: upload BEGIN … DESTRUCTIVE from the next write ::` → four `prologue N/4` lines → `prologue post … psm-run=0 want=0 MATCH` → `upload macctl-pre displaced=0x… wrote=0x00000404` → `ucode upload words=9938 … via SHM_CONTROL<-0x03000000` → `upload verify … => MATCH` → `upload psm start wrote=0x00020402 … ready=1` → `upload handshake shm[0x0,0x2,0x4,0x6]=[…]` → `:: wifi2: ucode upload words=9938 crc=0x… psm=1 rev=<r> -> UPLOADED ::`. Only THEN does WIFI4 FLIGHT's wire shape above become reachable. **⚠ PAST `upload BEGIN` THERE IS NO UNWIND** — the prologue reset clears `MACCTL.PSM_RUN` and destroys the EFI-resident microcode (§5 risk 4); the only exits are `-> UPLOADED` or a reboot. A `FAILED` verdict names its first failed leg (`prologue-psm-still-running`, `verify-mismatch`, `ready-timeout`, `rev-not-running`, `rev-below-floor`, `psm-not-running`, `psm-jmp0-still-set`) and the radio is dead until power-cycle — that is the known cost, not a surprise. **PETER'S GO IS REQUIRED for this sitting.** **AUDIT:** `wrote-core-regs` on a refusal now discriminates — `0` = returned before the probe, `1` = the probe alone and nothing destructive ran.
· A9  serial console is TX-only (FTDI bulk IN 0x81 never driven) — no typing over the wire (the x86 half of S29)
· A1  BAR1 wedge under paint bursts (`storm`): a core dies mid-blit
· NEW  BAR1WEDGE FLIGHT — **the A1 flight, and it is two boots under `storm`, not one.** Boot A is the EXPERIMENT: the rMBP line plus `UNAOS_BAR1EXP=uc UNAOS_BAR1WEDGE=1`. Boot B is its CONTROL: the same line with `UNAOS_BAR1WEDGE=1` and NO `UNAOS_BAR1EXP` — and boot B must WEDGE, or boot A proves only that the storm was weak. Both knobs on boot A or it is unscorable (the ~6.8x UC slowdown explains a quiet boot as readily as the theory does). Confirm `bar1exp-uc` AND `bar1wedge` in the `⚡ kernel features:` banner AND by `LC_ALL=C grep -a -o -F ':: BAR1WEDGE:'` on the artifact — s42's INSTGUI lesson, and this knob's entire product is witness lines, so a banner-only arm would read as a negative result about the hardware. THREE LINES at kepler init (`:: BAR1WEDGE: rung=first-stall …`, `[pcih] bar1wedge cto rp …`, `[pcih] bar1wedge sticky-cleared at-arm …`), then one `[pcih] wedge-sample n=…` per tripwire crossing beside the unchanged `[pcih] rp-at-wedge`. **A FOURTH BOOT LINE joined them (SECSTA2, register §6 P7, same knob — no new one): `[pcih] bar1wedge sticky-cleared post-enum rp=… secsta=…->… lnksta=…->… relatch=secsta:… lnksta:… at-arm=…` — NOT at kepler init but at the tail of `pci::init`, after the LAST bus walk of the boot, so the wedge-sample's `d_secsta`/`d_lnksta` delta against the end of enumeration instead of against the Kepler dispatch (`d_devsta` still deltas against at-arm, and the line says so). SCORE `relatch=` ON ITS OWN: `secsta:2000` means a bus walk on this bridge does latch Received Master Abort, and the `secsta=2000` of boots 8/9/11 is then fully explained without the wedge; `secsta:0000` means no walk latched it at all, and a `d_secsta=2000` at `n=1` has nothing left to blame but the wedge — a reading nobody has ever had, and the premise §1.3's whole residue argument assumes without measuring. This line ABSENT while `:: BAR1WEDGE:` is PRESENT means `pci::init` never reached its end, which is itself the finding.** **READ `n=1` — that is the first stall.** Absence controls: on boot A `fb-wc` ABSENT and `:: x86 bar1exp: UC arm ARMED` PRESENT, the reverse on boot B; `[pcih] rp-boot` and `[pcih] rp-at-wedge` present and unchanged in shape on BOTH. Read with `awk 'index($0,"[pcih]")'`, never bare `grep`. Every outcome and what it means for A1: `docs/dev/OS/08_VIDEO/PCIE-RP-RECOVERY.md` §11.3. Expect, in order and as the final bytes before the machine goes, EXACTLY THREE lines — measured on the QEMU fixture, not predicted: `[pwrreboot] reboot verb invoked — dispatching the platform mechanism`, `[pwrreboot] x86 mechanism: FADT RESET_REG ladder (acpi_power::reboot)`, `[pwrreboot] ftdi flushed bytes=B transfers=T exhausted=0`. ⚠ `[pwrreboot] ring drained lines=N bytes=M` will NOT be among them and its absence is not a failure: `power_drain` writes both the staged lines and its own tally through `arch::serial::raw_write_str`, which on x86 is the 16550 at 0x3F8 alone — it never enters the FTDI mirror ring, so on this laptop PWRDRAIN's whole output goes to a port that is not there. RESIDUAL GAP, found by this fixture and NOT fixed by RBTDRAIN: a line DEFERRED into the staging ring on a contended rMBP boot is consumed by `power_drain` into that dead port before the FTDI flush can ever see it. Closing it means giving `power_drain` a sink that is `_print`'s set and not one port (`serial_ring.rs`, outside this brief). `exhausted=1` does NOT close the row — it converts it into "the flush ran and the budget bound first", and the number to read next is `bytes=`. The FADT ladder's own `raw_witness` lines (`FADT RESET_REG space=… — writing`) will NOT appear on the cable and their absence is not a failure: `raw_witness` is `raw_write_str`, the 16550 this laptop does not have — that fact is A3 itself, and `reset_report`'s boot-time line (BOOTFADT, flown F7) is where those facts already are. Same verb from the desktop's Restart closes nothing here (x86 has no `crystal_restart`; the menu routes through `power::reboot`). Score `shutdown` on the same sitting, and the desktop's Shut Down too: the fold is on BOTH x86 power ports (`acpi_power::poweroff`'s signature is `s5_ring_flush(); ftdi_flush_sync(…)`), so `video/crystal.rs` and `video/instgui.rs`, which call `poweroff` DIRECTLY, must also end with `[pwrshutoff] ftdi flushed bytes=B transfers=T exhausted=0` as the cable's last line before the board goes dark. A dark board with NO such line is a red, not a known gap. `docs/dev/OS/02_KERNEL_CORE/serial_transport.md` §RBTDRAIN

On DisplayPort the same fields are **T3** (`PP_ON_DELAYS[28:16]`), **T9** (`PP_OFF_DELAYS[12:0]`), **T10** (`PP_OFF_DELAYS[28:16]`) and **T12** (`PP_DIVISOR[4:0]`) — and the units are not uniform: 100 µs for the first three, **100 ms** for T12. **TWO RUNGS LANDED, BOTH READ-ONLY** (no `write_volatile`, no `dp_aux_transfer` in either; no display-register write lands on this arc): `07b name=pps-read` decodes all five PPS registers and rolls up `panel_powered_by_fw=` — what a later `pps-on` must PRESERVE; `08b name=modeset-read` reads `DP_CTL_A`, takes the pipe from its own `[30:29]` Pipe Select, reads `PIPE_CONF` and the live M/N set from its bit 20, and prints the firmware's mode with a refresh derived from two cited fields. B125's timing verdict gets its mechanism. ⚠ **G12'S DECLINE STILL STANDS:** this retires leg (i) *no citation* only; legs (ii) *metal says the write is unnecessary* and (iii) *the delays read zero and a PPS on zero delays is the panel-damage path* are untouched, and no boot of this build raises panel power. ⚠ **Two traps recorded for the write rung:** §2.4.3 p.41 ties the `ABCD` key to `PP_ON_DELAYS[31:30]=01b DisplayPort A` while this machine reads `00b` LVDS with zero delays (half a sequencer, printed as `port_sel_conflict=`); and §2.4.2 p.39's write-protected list includes **HTOTAL/HBLANK/HSYNC/VTOTAL/VBLANK/VSYNC**, so a protected timing write "completes as normal" and changes nothing. ⚠ **`DP_TP_CTL`, `DP_TP_STATUS` and `TRANS_DDI_FUNC_CTL` do not exist on this silicon** — zero hits in either display volume; IVB trains eDP through `DP_CTL_A[9:8]` with the port CPU-attached, so no PCH transcoder is in the path. Rung 08's last line was rewritten (and only that line) because `hits=0` had stopped being true; every other token of it is byte-identical. `end` moves 09 → **10**; compare on `name=`, never the number. · NEW  KFUNWEDGE FLIGHT — ⚠⚠ **A SACRIFICIAL BOOT. THE OPERATOR MUST AGREE TO THE COST BEFORE THE SITTING IS PLANNED, NOT AFTER THE SCREEN GOES DARK.** The KF29 flight, ONE boot, and it is the only rung in this tree that DELIBERATELY BREAKS THE HARDWARE IT MEASURES: it performs one host READ of `0x409504` (`WRCMD_CMD`) on purpose, and `falcon_microcode_spec.md` §5.4 — the poison law, discovered s31, proved by its own control frame s32, convicted by elimination s34 — says the first access faults immediately and wedges every subsequent read in the FECS unit for the rest of the boot. **THE COST, stated plainly: the Kepler IS this machine's panel** (the x86 compositor's ignition is the Kepler takeover), so wedging its PRI ring may take the glass until a **POWER CYCLE**, not a reboot. The serial capture is the deliverable; a dark screen is the expected price, not a new failure. Have the power button ready. **FLY IT LAST IN THE SITTING AND ALONE** — never with BEAMX86, KDHEAD, `UNAOS_KEPLER_KFBIND` (KF27) or `UNAOS_KEPLER_KFCTXBIND` (KF28) aboard, because every verdict collected after this rung is void by §5.4 and each of those rungs would print its own conditions string over a boot this one had already poisoned. The code enforces the ordering INSIDE the boot (the call site is the last kepler statement before the terminal poke, so every proven read of the boot has completed and printed first); it cannot enforce the knob set, so the knob set is the operator's discipline and the flight line must be checked. **SEQUENCED AFTER the KFBIND FLIGHT row above** for the same reason KFCTXBIND is: the rung gates itself on a KF27-style base resolution and prints `skipped reason=kf27-base-unresolved` otherwise — which poisons NOTHING (the unit stays intact and the boot is not the sacrificial one) but spends the slot. Flight line: add `UNAOS_KEPLER_KFUNWEDGE=1` to the standing `UNAOS_KEPLER=1 UNAOS_KEPLER_FIFO=1` and change nothing else. The knob is NEW: it must be on the flight line AND in the `⚡ kernel features:` banner AND proven in the artifact by `LC_ALL=C grep -a -o -F 'KFUNWEDGE' target/x86_64_esp/kernel.elf | wc -l` (> 0), never `strings` — the s42/INSTGUI + BANNERCERT lesson, and here it matters more than on any other rung: a banner-only build would spend a sacrificial boot, and possibly the panel, on an image with no instrument in it. **FOUR LINES DECIDE IT**, in this order, every outcome table pre-registered in the PENDING METAL entry in `KEPLER-METAL-LOG.md`. (1) `:: KFUNWEDGE: base class=…` — not `DERIVED-WINS` or `BOTH-ANSWER` and the next two lines are the last; **nothing was measured and nothing was poisoned**. (2) `:: KFUNWEDGE: poke read=0x409504 value=… poisoned=<yes|no>` — `yes` means the experiment is live; `no` means **the poison law did not fire on this boot**, which is LOUDER than an un-wedge would have been (three sittings saw it fire on first access, so a clean read names a condition those three did not share) and the register is re-scored around it rather than the rung called a failure (R19). (3) `:: KFUNWEDGE: observe … unit_poisoned=… control_poisoned=…` — `unit_poisoned=Y control_poisoned=n` BOUNDS the damage to the FECS unit with a real `NV_PMC_BOOT_0` beside a `BADF` `cpuctl`, which is the reading s31 inferred from a PFIFO witness and never took directly; `control_poisoned=Y` is `-> VOID-CONTROL` and the boot says nothing. (4) `:: KFUNWEDGE: verdict … -> <…>` — `-> UNWEDGED` answers the question KF21 opened in July and makes every future probe of an unproven `0x409xxx` offset survivable, which is worth more than any single offset's value; `-> STILL-POISONED under {clear=written|skipped, …}` names its conditions and is INCOMPLETE rather than negative if `clear=skipped`; `-> POISON-CONFINED` contradicts s31/s32's spread and is a per-offset finding; `-> NOT-POISONED` is (2) again. ⚠ **The terminal poke's own datum is VOID on this boot** — `falcon_microcode_spec.md` §10 evidences that write by its being the boot's ONLY access to the offset, and here the read came first; the `fecs-ledger` line prints `504_read_idx`/`504_write_idx` so the capture states the ordering. Do NOT score `:: kepler: terminal-poke` on a KFUNWEDGE flight. Read with `awk 'index($0,":: KFUNWEDGE:")'`, never bare `grep`. Register §2 KF29

So the fixture that proves root-by-content on x86 IS the install-to-internal-disk configuration. MEASURED `UNAOS_WC=1 UNAOS_AHCI=1 UNAOS_QEMU_FULL=1 ./arroyo test 90` rc=0: `:: X86BIND: root=ahci5:/kernel.elf serial=0xfabe1afd by=content bootinfo=0xfabe1afd agrees=yes mounts=4 layout=true -> PASS ::`, `/` `/boot` `/apps` on one volume id (the ESP), `/volumes/UNAOS SDHC4` LISTED not bound, TSTE 24/0, `[vfs] resurvey n=1 bound_on_pass=2` (SO38's fix is what makes the second walk happen). FATVERB's `readvol` leg became a CENSUS, not an identity (`exec=sdhc read=ahci same=false` — two independent producers by `vfs.md` §13.4, entitled to differ). GO-REDS: re-latch the NONE -> `root=- … -> FAIL`, rc=1; flip the loader serial -> `agrees=no` and the verdict stays `-> PASS`. THE SEAM IS THE PRODUCT: `kepler::try_bind_and_witness(bar0, inst_off) -> BindWitness` at the tail of `kepler.rs`, carved out of `kepler::init` between the channel bind and the `PFIFO_CHAN[1] pre-submit` read, holding the three PFIFO_CHAN[1] writes, the 0x252c/0x263c bracket, the two channel reads and all four prints VERBATIM; `init` calls it once, `#[inline(always)]`, at a replacement site that is exactly as many lines (24) as the block it replaced. Per TARGET the rung writes the encoding (entry-captured by the audit, restored and read back by it four lines on), calls the seam, and prints the rollup. **R19 SEQUENCING IS IN CODE** (the KF28 precedent): it runs only where the audit's own `RO?` check said WRITABLE on THIS boot, else `skipped reason=ctrladdr-readonly` and it writes nothing (and `reason=ctrladdr-absent` where the PBDMA aperture did not answer). **RE-ENTRANT BY CONSTRUCTION** — audited and written down at the seam: no static, no once-cell, no `static mut`, no allocation, and deliberately no `fecs_read`/`fecs_write`, whose `FECS_ACCESS_COUNT`/`FECS_FIRST_OFFSET`/`FECS_504_*` cells have once semantics a re-entry would corrupt; its ONLY residue is PFIFO_CHAN[1] +0x00/+0x04, which the rung captures before the audit and puts back after it, READ BACK. **NO NEW REGISTER**: the seam writes and reads exactly the four words `init` writes and reads today at the site it was carved from. ⚠ **THE CARVE-OUT MOVES THE ARMED IMAGE and the number is here rather than hidden**: measured on the extraction ALONE, armed over `nvidia-kepler,nvidia-kepler-takeover,nvidia-kepler-fifo,nvidia-kepler-ctrladdr` with `llvm-objcopy -O binary` in one directory, the flat image goes 1,455,360 -> 1,455,868 bytes with **414 of it inside `kepler::init`** (`llvm-nm`: 0xa28b -> 0xa429); `pub`-vs-private and an 18-byte-vs-16-byte return were both BUILT and both refuted as the cause, and the disassembly of `init` filtered to this seam's operands gives the SAME 35 MMIO accesses in the SAME order in both images. The gated invariant holds: `./arroyo knoboff nvidia-kepler-ctrlbind` **exit 0** and `./arroyo knoboff nvidia-kepler-ctrladdr` **exit 0**, byte-identical to `4638014e` on both arches, control fired. **BUILD LINE FOR THE FLIGHT** (the rung needs all of these — `UNAOS_KEPLER_FIFO` is what makes its call site reachable at all): `UNAOS_WC=1 UNAOS_KEPLER=1 UNAOS_KEPLER_TAKEOVER=1 UNAOS_KEPLER_FIFO=1 UNAOS_KEPLER_CTRL_ADDR=1 UNAOS_KEPLER_CTRLBIND=1 ./arroyo esp-x86`, and confirm the instrument is IN THE IMAGE with `LC_ALL=C grep -a -o -F ':: kepler: ctrlbind ' <artifact>` before the boot, never by the `⚡ kernel features:` banner alone (s42's INSTGUI lesson, LAWS §5). **EXPECTED WIRE, and what scores it:** one `:: kepler: ctrlbind begin chan00=<8hex> chan04=<8hex> inst_off=<8hex> ::`, then per PBDMA (three) per TARGET (four) either `:: kepler: ctrlbind pbdma<i> target=<t> ctrl_addr=<8hex> bind=<ok|fail> witness=<latched|silent> chan00=<8hex> chan04=<8hex> sched=err=<8hex>,stat=<8hex> ::` or a `skipped reason=` line in its place, then one `:: kepler: ctrlbind chan-restored 00=<8hex>(want <8hex>) 04=<8hex>(want <8hex>) verdict=<clean|DIRTY> ::`. **THE QUESTION THE FLIGHT ANSWERS IS ONE FIELD: `witness=`.** Flight 10's unarmed baseline is `PFIFO_CHAN[1] pre-submit: 00=00002000 04=11000001` with `sched-status post-init err=00000002` — the VALID|POLL_ENABLE bits stripped — so twelve `witness=silent` rows reproduce the strip under every TARGET encoding and CLOSE s13's question (shut-out, never "ruled out": the conditions are on the line beside it). **ONE `witness=latched` is the campaign's first channel validate and outranks everything else on the boot.** ⚠ A CTRLBIND boot carries THIRTEEN copies of `sched-status post-init`, `H3 arm=` and `PFIFO_CHAN[1] pre-submit` — the seam prints them, deliberately, so a re-entry sees the same MMIO/serial interleaving `init` sees; the LAST copy of each is `init`'s own, and the `ctrlbind` rollups are what to score. ⚠ QEMU PROVES NOTHING HERE: it has no Kepler, the run is green with the path declining, and the armed-image delta above is explained but not wire-proven until this flight. Register §2 KF9 + KF9b, §7 KF9 (now **restored in full**). **METAL DONE — FLOWN AS FLIGHT 11, 2026-09-22, AND THE VERDICT IS: NO TARGET ENCODING CHANGES THE STRIP.** The wire came out exactly in the shape this row predicted, which is worth one line because the row wrote the shape BEFORE the boot: `:: kepler: ctrlbind begin chan00=00000000 chan04=00000000 inst_off=02000000 ::`, then twelve rollups — pbdma0-2 × target 0-3, **no `skipped reason=` line anywhere**, so all twelve encodings read WRITABLE and the in-code R19 gate let every one run — each `ctrl_addr=0000000<t> bind=ok witness=silent chan00=00002000 chan04=11000001 sched=err=00000002,stat=00000000`, then `:: kepler: ctrlbind chan-restored 00=00000000(want 00000000) 04=00000001(want 00000000) verdict=DIRTY ::`. **THE ONE FIELD THIS ROW SAID WOULD DECIDE IT, `witness=`, READ `silent` TWELVE TIMES OUT OF TWELVE.** The witness latches on a channel VALIDATE and not one of the twelve produced one, so s13's actual question is ANSWERED and §2's KF9 closes as **shut-out by measurement** — not "ruled out": flight 11 ran on the same never-ran-FECS host as s13, so the condition §2's KF6/KF8/KF9 bullet names is UNVARIED and KF9 stays on §9's twenty-one-rung R19 list for that reason alone. The thirteen-copy warning held too: thirteen `sched-status post-init` / `H3 arm=` / `PFIFO_CHAN[1] pre-submit` triples, the last `init`'s own at `00=00002000 04=11000001`, byte-identical to the twelve — the re-entrancy audit's no-accumulating-state claim, measured. ⚠ **THE BOOT ALSO RETURNED A DEFECT IN THE RESTORE, and it is closed by KF9CLOSE (`exec-rmbp-kf9close`, parent `b9edcfa1`, ledger B133):** `chan04` came back `00000001` against an entry `00000000`. No write of this rung ever carried bit 0 — the chip sets it when the VALID bind word goes into `+0x00` — and the restore's `+0x04=0` cleared `11000000` and not bit 0 because it landed while the channel was STILL BOUND: the restore wrote `+0x04` first and released `+0x00` second, copying the bringup's "VALID written last" rule into its own undoing, where it is backwards. Fixed by ORDER alone — release `+0x00` first, put `+0x04` back LAST, read both — and the line now prints `order=00-then-04` with a residue-NAMING verdict `<clean\|DIRTY-chanstate\|DIRTY>`. **OWED: one more boot on the SAME knob line, to print which of `clean` and `DIRTY-chanstate` is true.** `DIRTY-chanstate` would mean the release does not drop the bit either — a latch no write clears — which is a finding, not a failure, and the old undifferentiated `DIRTY` could not have told the two apart. QEMU cannot settle it: no Kepler. Register §2 KF9 + KF9b, §7 KF9 (**restored in full and flown**), §9
