✓ DONE (code) / ✓ FLOWN  KDHEAD  **METAL DONE, KD14 PROVEN**: `blocks=5 separated=2 decoded=1 agree=1 disagree=0 writes=0`; `mirror` separates (`heads_distinct=2/4 stable=4/4`), `armed100` collapses (`1/4 stable=3/3` — s4's inference is now an observation), and head 0 `-> AGREE` pins `mirror`'s slicing by observation and **RE-OPENS KD3** (flights 8+9, 2026-09-16; docs/dev/evidence/rmbp-0916/flight8-9/FLIGHT8-9.md) — `exec-rmbp-kdhead` — built as register §1 rung **KD14 `kdhead`** (`drivers/gpu/kepler_display.rs`, file tail; knob `UNAOS_KEPLER_KDHEAD=1`, feature `nvidia-kepler-kdhead` implying `nvidia-kepler` + `nvidia-kepler-takeover`, DEFAULT OFF, **`writes=0`** — every device access is `mmio_read`, there is no `mmio_write` and no `write_volatile` in the rung). This row asked for KD3's per-head EVO/CRTC decode re-run with KD4's `head[0] stat underflow=` as the control bracket and the per-head stride re-derived, and that is what landed — but TWO of its three parts came out differently from the wording, and both differences are the design. **(1) THE BRACKET IS BORROWED, NOT RE-TAKEN.** BEAMX86's `beam_probe` already samples `HEAD_STAT.VERT` on all four heads for 45 ms apiece; it now PUBLISHES that per-head census and KD14 reads it. A second sampler would have cost another 180 ms inside the takeover and — the real objection — would have been a DIFFERENT sample than the one the beam gate armed on, so any disagreement between the two would have been unattributable. `live = adv>0 && vbd>=2`, BEAMX86's own test. Consequence for the flight: **`UNAOS_BEAM=1` rides the same boot**, and a boot without it prints `bracket=absent`, withholds every decode, and says on its own wire that it is not evidence about the hardware — which is KD3's shut-out condition refusing to repeat itself. **(2) THE STRIDE TEST HAS A TRAP THE ROW DID NOT NAME, AND IT POINTS THE WRONG WAY.** If a stride collapses, all four 'heads' are one register — and four reads of one COUNTER, taken microseconds apart, come back different and score `heads_distinct=4/4`: a broken stride reading as a working one. The bank KD3 and KD4 share holds exactly such counters (`+0x340` VERT, `+0x344` HORZ, the frame counter at `+0x314`, [METAL s13]). So none of them is probed, every block is read TWICE with a settle, and any word that MOVED is struck from the distinctness tuple; `stable=` reports how many survived, and a block where none survived scores `heads_distinct=0/4` — impossible for a real reading, since a head is always distinct from nothing, so the zero is the unambiguous not-scored marker (`verdict=UNSCORABLE-all-words-volatile` on the same line). Conversely a literal ZERO is a legal reading and is never counted as absent — that is KD3's error in the other direction; only `0xFFFFFFFF` and the `0xBADxxxxx` family fail to answer, and `readable=` reports them. **FIVE CANDIDATE BLOCKS, every one named by the tree or by a sitting:** `headstat` `0x616000`/`0x800` ([EXT] g80_pdisplay.xml:647, KD4's own bank [METAL s11], probed at the four STABLE words [METAL s12+s13] pinned), `armed100` `0x616100`/`0x800` ([METAL s4] — sitting #4's three offsets verbatim, the reading this rung re-takes; the field ROLES there are [UNPINNED] by s4's own words), `evocore` `0x610460`/`0x300` ([TREE] candidate A, layout [EXT] nv_evo.xml, the MMIO mirroring [UNPINNED]), `headval` `0x610A00`/`0x540` ([TREE] candidate B, [EXT] g80_pdisplay.xml:371-408 but G80:GF119-marked so [UNPINNED] on GK107), and `mirror` `0x640400`/`0x300` — the EVO core method mirror, **the one block whose FIELDS are metal-decoded ([METAL s16] `+0x20`/`+0x60`, [METAL s25]/KD8 `+0x68`/`+0x6C`) and whose STRIDE never has been**, because s16 and s25 both read head 0's record and nothing else. **(3) THE DECODE runs only where the stride separated heads AND the bracket says the head is live**, and every slicing carries its own citation class on the wire (§0.1: [TREE]/[METAL sN]/[EXT]/[UNPINNED]) — `headstat` deliberately decodes to `decode=none reason=no-cited-slicing`, because [METAL s13] settled that bank in the negative and `+0x34C` is raster TOTALS [METAL s13], not active geometry, and inventing a slicing there would be KD3's error a second time. Comparison is against the GOP framebuffer the takeover inherited, printed on its own `:: KDHEAD: gop …` line so the reference is in the capture. Falsifier, three-valued: `-> AGREE` on a live head pins that block's slicing BY OBSERVATION and re-opens KD3; `-> DISAGREE` names the field (`mismatch=geom|surface|pitch`) and is a finding about the SLICING, not the silicon; `separated=0` on the `end` line says every candidate stride collapses — s4's inference confirmed by measurement. ⚠ **None of the three is a statement that a head is dead**; the bracket line answers that independently, above the decode (R19, never 'ruled out'). Wired in all four places (arroyo map at the END of the kepler block + the KFCTXBIND 'LAST line' comment corrected, `builder/src/main.rs` read, `k8-reach.registry` NA row with `--evidence`, `x86-all` naming the feature at the END of its list); `arm_features` NOT touched — neither implied feature is in its strip list, re-checked. WHY IT GOES FIRST, and it is upstream of KF18/KF19 rather than a detour from them: every PBDMA observable a bind would be scored by is read through ONE unproven number. `kepler.rs` addresses the units at `0x40000 + i*0x2000`, a guess adopted at s#3 in the same sitting the previous guess (`0x6c0`) returned `0xBAD0011F`; at that base `bad-read pbdma 40108` returned POISON at s#4 and ZERO at s#5 — same address, same part — and `DISCRIMINATOR … ch=00000000 (CHID=0 ACTIVE=0)` has been quoted as a scheduler verdict since s#6. **A zero read at an unproven base is "this address returned zero", not "the channel is not scheduled".** Sittings #4 and #5 each closed asking for exactly this derivation and neither ever did it. KF11→KF14 is the precedent: four recorded failures whose causes were PMC bit 12 clear, the wrong base, the wrong base again, and DMACTL REQUIRE_CTX — not one a property of the silicon. SECOND HALF, and it needed no metal to find: `kepler.rs`'s beacon-plant argument quotes envytools `docs/hw/fifo/dma-pusher.rst` "Channel control area" (`… IB_GET 0x88, IB_PUT 0x8C`) and then records, two lines on, that this driver's deleted GP_GET/GP_PUT witness read **0x8C/0x90** — IB_PUT and a word the list does not name. **`gp_get=0`, the campaign's founding observable, may never have been a reading of GP_GET**, and nothing in this tree reads `0x88`. ✓ DONE (code) / · NEW METAL  KFUNWEDGE  `exec-rmbp-kfunwedge` — built as register §2 rung **KF29 `kfunwedge`** (`drivers/gpu/kepler_fifo.rs`, `mod unwedge`, beside KF27 and KF28; knob `UNAOS_KEPLER_KFUNWEDGE=1`, feature `nvidia-kepler-kfunwedge` implying `nvidia-kepler` + `nvidia-kepler-fifo`, DEFAULT OFF). ⚠⚠ **IT IS NOT READ-ONLY AND IT IS NOT A NORMAL RUNG: IT IS A SACRIFICIAL BOOT.** Every other rung this seat has landed reports `writes=0 restored=n/a`; this one performs ONE deliberate host read of `0x409504` (`WRCMD_CMD`) and up to two W1C write-backs, and reports **`restored=IMPOSSIBLE`** — there is no write that un-reads a read. **THE OPERATOR'S COST, and it belongs in the sitting plan rather than the postmortem:** the Kepler IS this machine's panel (the x86 compositor's ignition is the Kepler takeover), so wedging its PRI ring on purpose may take the glass until a **POWER CYCLE**, not a reboot; the serial capture is the deliverable and a dark screen is the expected price. **FLY IT LAST IN A SITTING AND ALONE** — never with BEAMX86, KDHEAD, KF27 or KF28 aboard, since every verdict collected after this rung is void by §5.4. The code enforces the ORDERING inside the boot (the call site is the last kepler statement before the terminal poke, the §5.4 rule taken literally: every proven read of the boot has completed and printed first); it cannot enforce the knob set, which is why the rule is written here, in register §2 KF29, and in `KEPLER-METAL-LOG.md`'s PENDING entry. **WHY IT IS OWED AND WHY THE CLEAR WAS NEVER THE MISSING PIECE.** §2's KF21 row has said the same sentence for ten sittings: the poison register is *writable* without consequence (s41's `terminal-poke 0x409504 wr=0` → `[NVIDIA] Initialization complete`), *reading* it is what poisons, and the un-wedge experiment remains UNEXERCISED because nothing has ever wedged on a boot that went looking. The observe-and-clear chain was designed and LANDED once — pull 30 (`0e26447e`) — and flew at s34, where all five probed offsets read clean, so the un-wedge half never executed and the code was later removed; s33boot1 recorded the identical miss. **What was missing was a wedge that fires ON PURPOSE**, at a point in the boot where losing the unit costs nothing. FIVE STEPS. (1) BASELINE, read-only: the PRING fault words this tree has cited — `PBUS_INTR` (`0x1100`, **[METAL s33]**, read `0000000C` on this part and W1C'd in that boot) and the PIBUS trio `0x120120`/`0x120124`/`0x120128` (**[METAL s33]** + **[EXT]** names, all read real `00000000`), plus `PIBUS_MMIO_HUB_ENABLE1` (`0x122104`, **[METAL s33]**) as context — beside `cpuctl` (**[METAL s26/s28/s31/s34]**), `mailbox0` (**[METAL s29]**) and the FECS access ledger, so the wire proves nothing above had already poisoned the unit. (2) THE POKE: exactly ONE read of `0x409504` (**[METAL s31/s32/s34]**), through `kepler::fecs_read` so the boot's own FECS accounting counts it, printed with its value and `poisoned=<yes|no>`. (3) OBSERVE: the ring re-read with bits 2 `MMIO_RING_ERR` / 3 `MMIO_FAULT` tagged **[EXT]**, `cpuctl` and `mailbox0` re-read, and `NV_PMC_BOOT_0` — OUTSIDE the unit — as the control, which is what BOUNDS the damage: a real chip ID beside a `BADF1000` `cpuctl` says the FECS unit, a poisoned one says the whole BAR0 path and the boot is `-> VOID-CONTROL`. s31 inferred that bound from a PFIFO witness line; this reads it in the same breath. (4) CLEAR: a W1C of EXACTLY the bits read as SET one line above — **writing back the exact value just read is the least-assumptive write available**: a clear in a W1C register, a no-op by construction in a plain RW one, asserting no field layout and no bit meaning beyond "these bits were set a microsecond ago", which is an observation of this boot and not a citation. A register reading ZERO is not written at all (`skipped write=pring_clear_<reg> reason=nothing-latched`); `INTR_ADDR`/`INTR_VALUE` are never written, because they report which access faulted and are not documented as latches. (5) RE-READ and score: `-> UNWEDGED` / `-> STILL-POISONED under {clear=…}` / `-> POISON-CONFINED` / `-> NOT-POISONED` / `-> VOID-BRACKET` / `-> VOID-CONTROL`, every arm naming its conditions (R19, never "ruled out"). **R19 GATE ON KF27, enforced in code**, exactly as KF28's is: the rung re-runs KF27's base ladder read-only over KF27's own PTOP address, extraction, window and probe offsets and prints `skipped reason=kf27-base-unresolved` unless it resolves `DERIVED-WINS` or `BOTH-ANSWER` — poisoning NOTHING on that path. It deliberately implies NEITHER sibling feature, so the co-flight ban is a runtime and operator matter and never a Cargo one. ⚠ **It VOIDS the terminal poke's own datum on an armed boot** (`falcon_microcode_spec.md` §10 evidences that write by its being the boot's only access to the offset, and here the read came first); the `fecs-ledger` line prints `504_read_idx`/`504_write_idx` so the capture states the ordering. ⚠ **STILL NOT TOUCHED BY BAR1WEDGE**, and the distinction this row has always carried stands: KFUNWEDGE is the FIFO/PBDMA ladder's un-wedge (register §2, a GK107 PRI poison recovered inside the endpoint); BAR1WEDGE is the PCIe ladder's wedge INSTRUMENT (register §6, root-port config only). Register §2 KF29; PENDING METAL entry in `KEPLER-METAL-LOG.md`. OWED: the flight — see the METAL row above

· FLAKEFIX (branch `exec-rmbp-flakefix`, parent `50571edb`; the branch tip is the sha — the seat fills it at the fold) — **the 2026-09-22 flake day, measured: three reds, three different mechanisms, one fixed and two specified.** rmbp-ledger **B150**; corpus entries `docs/dev/FIXTURE_FLAKES.md` Class 7a (new), §2a (rewritten), §2b (new), §1d (new), recurrence rows on §1c and Class 3. **(1) FIXED, host-side only:** `x86-ptr.spec`'s `installs=36 reports=36` was measuring the host's scheduler — QEMU's `hid_pointer_event` folds motion events the guest has not polled, this lane alternates +24/-24 so the fold is exactly zero and the guest discards it, and the tell (`:: MOUSE-1: … last dx=0 dy=0`, a delta the typist cannot send) was printed on every capture and read by nobody. `scripts/qmp_type.py --pointer-ack` now paces on the guest's own count and re-sends exactly the shortfall; go-red `installs=8` -> four consecutive `installs=36 reports=36` at loads 10-41. Two host-side go-reds: `UNAOS_PTRLANE_NOACK=1` and `UNAOS_PTRLANE_GAP=<secs>`. **(2) OWED, `drivers/xhci/ftdi.rs`:** SERWIT-2's `evidence_lost` is the FTDI mirror's 64-slot staging ring hitting depth under lock contention (`staged=64/65 dropped=7/13 torn=0` on the FAILs, `staged=10..30 dropped=0` on the PASSes); `torn=0` everywhere refutes the per-line-growth suspect, and there is no QEMU chardev in the path at all. `mirror()` declares a line lost after ONE free `try_lock` retry — give it the back-pressure policy SERWIT-1B already proves on the same boot. NOT a wider tolerance. **And §2a's disposition was wrong in one sentence, now corrected: SERWIT-2 DOES red the gate**, through `FAULT_PATTERNS`, not through any spec. **(3) OWED, `video/dock.rs`:** DOCKID `order=false set=false` is a Class-1 registry re-read race (the two legs reading local scratch pass in 5/5 sightings, both legs reading the shared registry fail in 5/5), and it is ON METAL as well as under QEMU — flights 8 and 11 carry it, which is why FIXTURE_FLAKES' blanket "none of these classes has been seen on metal" is corrected in this arc, NOT the lost-input mechanism of (1) — proved by a capture where the typist's stream folded 36 -> 8 and DOCKID passed in the same boot. Cure is Class 1's published-flag-and-park, not a retry and not a widened assert. **Also banked:** `[dmgovlp] drag_evt=0 … adopt_stretch=0/4 -> FAIL` is no longer 'seen once' — 2 reds in the day's 6 x86 battery runs (`video/wm.rs`, PTRPAINT's file, §1c). **RATES, measured over every QEMU boot on the bench that day, de-duplicated per boot:** dmgovlp 6/47 (~13%), SERWIT-2 2/65 (~3%, against the ~8% the corpus estimated), DOCKID 2/46 (~4%) plus 3 metal sightings. **COST PRICED:** nine executors x one re-run x ~25 min ≈ 3.75 machine-hours in one day. NO KERNEL BYTE MOVED, so no `knoboff` verdict is quoted. OWED: the two fixes above, each to whoever next holds that file
· FLAKEFIX2 (branch `exec-rmbp-flakefix2`, parent `acd102d7`; the branch tip is the sha — the seat fills it at the fold; ledger row **B159**) — **FLAKEFIX's two owed fixes, re-opened on the wire: one is MADE AND UNSCORED, the other is NOT MADE because the capture refutes the fix it was to execute.** **(A) `drivers/xhci/ftdi.rs` — MADE.** `mirror()` now takes the primary wire's own contended-producer policy (`serial_ring::defer_policy`, compiler-checked rows, bounded by `BACKPRESSURE_SPINS`) instead of declaring a line lost after ONE free `try_lock`; each turn re-tries the SINK first, nothing is held across a turn, panic mode collapses the bound to 1 (= the old single retry, turn for turn), `evidence_lost`'s threshold unchanged at zero. LINE-NEUTRAL (843 lines before and after). **UNSCORED, and that is the row's news:** §2a's proof is a run at `staged >= 64` with `dropped=0`, and four `./arroyo test` boots read the `ftdi` tap at `staged=0/1/0/0` — below even the `staged=10..30` of §2a's own PASS captures. Load 29.58 -> 56.98, six sibling `cargo` builds, and a run with five vCPUs pinned onto four spinning host CPUs moved it 0 -> 1. **The changed branch was never taken**, so the four greens are NO-REGRESSION only, and the go-red is unreachable for the same reason. OWED: a deliberate widener that fills `STAGE` on demand — every place one can live (`serial_ring`'s SERWIT fixtures, or an `arroyo`+`builder` knob) is outside this brief's file set, which is why it is not here. ⚠ SERIALTX is in flight on `arch/x86_64/serial.rs`, the file `mirror()` is called from, and HAS moved the contention shape — its capture reads `ftdi: … staged=7` with `290 deferred … 30 back-pressured`, a deeper primary-ring shape than this parent produces; these four runs are on `acd102d7`, which does not contain it. **(B) `video/dock.rs` — NOT MADE, DIAGNOSIS CORRECTED.** §1d read DOCKID `order=false set=false` as a reader-side Class-1 re-read race. The legs and the end state are right; the stale snapshot is the **WRITER'S**. On a QEMU FAIL the reconcile admits `idA`/`idB` and not `idC` (allocated moments earlier), publishes a census with `idC` live and tileless, and the NEXT pass admits `idD` at `seq=17` and `idC` at `seq=18` — **the elder window given the later rank**, where a green boot of the same six windows gives `idC` 17 and `idD` 18. A tile's rank is thus the arrival of the first reconcile pass that SAW it, not the window's own arrival. **A reader-side seqlock cannot fix that**: the census is complete, consistent and stable — not torn, just permanently wrong. **And the three metal sightings are CLASS 6, not Class 1**: across the whole fixture on flight 11 there is not one `[dock] tile add`/`remove`/`census` line while six windows open and close, and `dock::compose` reconciles unconditionally and prints on every admit — so no reconcile ran at all, the fixture's `wm::composite()` DECLINED, and `strip_model` scored a registry no pass ever wrote. OWED, priced separately: (1) `reconcile` must take its model when it MUTATES, not be handed one scanned before — a change to where a rank comes from, touching the single-writer rule, and needing a DELIBERATE widener (a `witness`-gated hook in `settle` minting a window inside the scan->reconcile gap, driven by `dockid_selftest`) because 2-in-46 shows nothing in three runs; (2) `strip_model` must read whether the composite it drove actually reconciled and SKIP when it did not — small, deterministic, and it clears all three metal sightings without widening an assertion. Corpus: `docs/dev/FIXTURE_FLAKES.md` §2a and §1d, both rewritten with the captures quoted line by line
